<p><b>American Samoa</b> (<a href="/wiki/Samoan_language" title="Samoan language">Samoan</a>: <i lang="sm">Amerika Sāmoa</i>, <small>pronounced&#160;</small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="und-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA" title="Help:IPA">[aˈmɛɾika ˈsaːmʊa]</a></span>; also <i><span title="Samoan-language text"><i lang="sm">Amelika Sāmoa</i></span></i> or <i><span title="Samoan-language text"><i lang="sm">Sāmoa Amelika</i></span></i>) is an <a href="/wiki/Territories_of_the_United_States" title="Territories of the United States">unincorporated territory of the United States</a> located in the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ocean" title="Pacific Ocean">South Pacific Ocean</a>, southeast of the <a href="/wiki/Island_country" title="Island country">island country</a> of <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> Its location is centered on <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1073938472">.mw-parser-output .geo-default,.mw-parser-output .geo-dms,.mw-parser-output .geo-dec{display:inline}.mw-parser-output .geo-nondefault,.mw-parser-output .geo-multi-punct{display:none}.mw-parser-output .longitude,.mw-parser-output .latitude{white-space:nowrap}</style><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="//geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=American_Samoa&amp;params=14.3_S_170.7_W_region:AS_type:isle"><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">14°18′S</span> <span class="longitude">170°42′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">14.3°S 170.7°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">-14.3; -170.7</span></span></span></a></span>. It is east of the <a href="/wiki/International_Date_Line" title="International Date Line">International Date Line</a>, while Samoa is west of the Line. The total land area is 199 square kilometers (76.8&#160;sq&#160;mi), slightly more than <a href="/wiki/Washington,_D.C." title="Washington, D.C.">Washington, D.C.</a> American Samoa is the southernmost territory of the United States and one of two U.S. territories south of the Equator, along with the uninhabited <a href="/wiki/Jarvis_Island" title="Jarvis Island">Jarvis Island</a>. Tuna products are the main exports, and the main trading partner is the rest of the United States.
</p><p>American Samoa consists of five main islands and two coral <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atolls</a>. The largest and most populous island is <a href="/wiki/Tutuila" title="Tutuila">Tutuila</a>, with the <a href="/wiki/Manu%CA%BBa_Islands" title="Manuʻa Islands">Manuʻa Islands</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rose_Atoll" title="Rose Atoll">Rose Atoll</a> and <a href="/wiki/Swains_Island" title="Swains Island">Swains Island</a> also included in the territory. All islands except for Swains Island are part of the <a href="/wiki/Samoan_Islands" title="Samoan Islands">Samoan Islands</a>, west of the <a href="/wiki/Cook_Islands" title="Cook Islands">Cook Islands</a>, north of <a href="/wiki/Tonga" title="Tonga">Tonga</a>, and some 500 kilometres (310&#160;mi) south of <a href="/wiki/Tokelau" title="Tokelau">Tokelau</a>. To the west are the islands of the <a href="/wiki/Wallis_and_Futuna" title="Wallis and Futuna">Wallis and Futuna</a> group. As of 2022, the population of American Samoa is approximately 45,443 people.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> Most American Samoans are bilingual and can speak <a href="/wiki/American_English" title="American English">English</a> and <a href="/wiki/Samoan_language" title="Samoan language">Samoan</a> fluently.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-census-social_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-social-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>American Samoa has been a member of the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Community" title="Pacific Community">Pacific Community</a> since 1983. American Samoa is noted for having the highest rate of military enlistment of any U.S. state or territory. As of September 9, 2014, the local <a href="/wiki/United_States_Army" title="United States Army">U.S. Army</a> recruiting station in <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago" title="Pago Pago">Pago Pago</a> was ranked first in production out of the 885 Army recruiting stations and centers under the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Army_Recruiting_Command" title="United States Army Recruiting Command">United States Army Recruiting Command</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>American Samoa is the only permanently inhabited territory of the United States in which <a href="/wiki/U.S._citizenship" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. citizenship">citizenship</a> is not granted at birth, and people born there are considered "<a href="/wiki/American_Samoan_citizenship_and_nationality" title="American Samoan citizenship and nationality">non-citizen nationals</a>".
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r886046785">.mw-parser-output .toclimit-2 .toclevel-1 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-3 .toclevel-2 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-4 .toclevel-3 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-5 .toclevel-4 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-6 .toclevel-5 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-7 .toclevel-6 ul{display:none}</style><div class="toclimit-4"><meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" /></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:402px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Samoa_islands_2002.gif" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Samoa_islands_2002.gif/400px-Samoa_islands_2002.gif" decoding="async" width="400" height="235" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Samoa_islands_2002.gif/600px-Samoa_islands_2002.gif 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/8/8f/Samoa_islands_2002.gif 2x" data-file-width="710" data-file-height="418" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Samoa_islands_2002.gif" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Samoa Islands</div></div></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Samoa" title="History of Samoa">History of Samoa</a>, <a href="/wiki/History_of_American_Samoa" title="History of American Samoa">History of American Samoa</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Tui_Manu%27a" title="Tui Manu&#39;a">Tui Manu'a</a></div>
<p>Traditional <a href="/wiki/Oral_literature" title="Oral literature">oral literature</a> of Samoa and Manuʻa talks of a widespread <a href="/wiki/Polynesians" title="Polynesians">Polynesian</a> network or <a href="/wiki/Confederation" title="Confederation">confederacy</a> (or "empire") that was prehistorically ruled by the successive Tui Manuʻa dynasties. Manuan genealogies and religious oral literature also suggest that the Tui Manuʻa had long been one of the most prestigious and powerful <a href="/wiki/Paramount_chief" title="Paramount chief">paramounts</a> of Samoa. Oral history suggests that the Tui Manuʻa kings governed a confederacy of far-flung islands which included <a href="/wiki/Fiji" title="Fiji">Fiji</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tonga" title="Tonga">Tonga</a><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> as well as smaller western Pacific <a href="/wiki/Chiefdom" title="Chiefdom">chiefdoms</a> and <a href="/wiki/Polynesian_outliers" class="mw-redirect" title="Polynesian outliers">Polynesian outliers</a> such as <a href="/wiki/Uvea_(Wallis_and_Futuna)" title="Uvea (Wallis and Futuna)">Uvea</a>, <a href="/wiki/Futuna_Island,_Wallis_and_Futuna" class="mw-redirect" title="Futuna Island, Wallis and Futuna">Futuna</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tokelau" title="Tokelau">Tokelau</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Tuvalu" title="Tuvalu">Tuvalu</a>. Commerce and exchange routes between the western Polynesian societies are well documented and it is speculated that the Tui Manuʻa dynasty grew through its success in obtaining control over the oceanic trade of currency goods such as finely woven ceremonial mats, whale <a href="/wiki/Ivory" title="Ivory">ivory</a> "<a href="/wiki/Tabua" title="Tabua">tabua</a>", <a href="/wiki/Obsidian" title="Obsidian">obsidian</a> and <a href="/wiki/Basalt" title="Basalt">basalt</a> tools, chiefly red feathers, and seashells reserved for royalty (such as polished <a href="/wiki/Nautilus" title="Nautilus">nautilus</a> and the egg <a href="/wiki/Cowry" class="mw-redirect" title="Cowry">cowry</a>).
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="18th_century:_First_Western_contact">18th century: First Western contact</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: 18th century: First Western contact">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Contact with Europeans began in the early 18th century. Dutchman <a href="/wiki/Jacob_Roggeveen" title="Jacob Roggeveen">Jacob Roggeveen</a> was the first known European to sight the <a href="/wiki/Samoan_Islands" title="Samoan Islands">Samoan Islands</a> in 1722, calling them the "Baumann Islands" after one of his captains. The next explorer to visit the islands was <a href="/wiki/Louis-Antoine_de_Bougainville" class="mw-redirect" title="Louis-Antoine de Bougainville">Louis-Antoine de Bougainville</a>, who named them the "Îles des Navigateurs" in 1768. British explorer <a href="/wiki/James_Cook" title="James Cook">James Cook</a> recorded the island names in 1773, but never visited.<sup id="cite_ref-Keating_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Keating-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 1789 visit by <a href="/wiki/Jean-Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Galaup,_comte_de_Lap%C3%A9rouse" title="Jean-François de Galaup, comte de Lapérouse">La Pérouse</a> ended in <a href="/wiki/Jean-Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Galaup,_comte_de_Lap%C3%A9rouse#South_Pacific" title="Jean-François de Galaup, comte de Lapérouse">an attack</a>, on a <a href="/wiki/Tutuila" title="Tutuila">Tutuila</a> water collection expedition, resulting in the death of his second in command <a href="/wiki/Paul_Antoine_Fleuriot_de_Langle" title="Paul Antoine Fleuriot de Langle">Capt. de Langle</a> and several of his crew. La Pérouse named the island "Massacre Island", and the bay near <a href="/wiki/Aasu,_American_Samoa" class="mw-redirect" title="Aasu, American Samoa">Aasu</a> is still called "Massacre Bay".<sup id="cite_ref-Keating_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Keating-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/HMS_Pandora_(1779)" title="HMS Pandora (1779)">HMS <i>Pandora</i></a>, under the command of Admiral Edward Edwards (Royal Navy officer), visited the island in 1791 during its search for the <a href="/wiki/Mutiny_on_the_Bounty" title="Mutiny on the Bounty">H.M.S. <i>Bounty</i> mutineers</a>. <a href="/wiki/Otto_von_Kotzebue" title="Otto von Kotzebue">Von Kotzebue</a> visited in 1824.<sup id="cite_ref-Keating_11-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Keating-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="19th_century">19th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: 19th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:German,_British,_American_warships_in_Apia_harbour,_Samoa_1899.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/German%2C_British%2C_American_warships_in_Apia_harbour%2C_Samoa_1899.jpg/220px-German%2C_British%2C_American_warships_in_Apia_harbour%2C_Samoa_1899.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="180" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/German%2C_British%2C_American_warships_in_Apia_harbour%2C_Samoa_1899.jpg/330px-German%2C_British%2C_American_warships_in_Apia_harbour%2C_Samoa_1899.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/German%2C_British%2C_American_warships_in_Apia_harbour%2C_Samoa_1899.jpg/440px-German%2C_British%2C_American_warships_in_Apia_harbour%2C_Samoa_1899.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3390" data-file-height="2776" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:German,_British,_American_warships_in_Apia_harbour,_Samoa_1899.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>German, British and American warships in Apia Harbor, Samoa, 1899</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Samoan_crisis" title="Samoan crisis">Samoan crisis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Samoan_Civil_War" title="Samoan Civil War">Samoan Civil War</a>, <a href="/wiki/Second_Samoan_Civil_War" title="Second Samoan Civil War">Second Samoan Civil War</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Tripartite_Convention" title="Tripartite Convention">Tripartite Convention</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Christian_mission" title="Christian mission">Mission work</a> in the Samoas had begun in late 1830 when <a href="/wiki/John_Williams_(missionary)" title="John Williams (missionary)">John Williams</a> of the <a href="/wiki/London_Missionary_Society" title="London Missionary Society">London Missionary Society</a> arrived from the <a href="/wiki/Cook_Islands" title="Cook Islands">Cook Islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> By the late nineteenth century, French, British, German, and American vessels routinely stopped at Samoa, as they valued <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago" title="Pago Pago">Pago Pago Harbor</a> as a refueling station for coal-fired shipping and whaling.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/United_States_Exploring_Expedition" title="United States Exploring Expedition">United States Exploring Expedition</a> visited the islands in 1839.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Samoa_Cram_Map_1896.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Samoa_Cram_Map_1896.jpg/220px-Samoa_Cram_Map_1896.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Samoa_Cram_Map_1896.jpg/330px-Samoa_Cram_Map_1896.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Samoa_Cram_Map_1896.jpg/440px-Samoa_Cram_Map_1896.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2165" data-file-height="1621" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Samoa_Cram_Map_1896.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>1896 map of the Samoa Islands</div></div></div>
<p>In March 1889, an <a href="/wiki/German_Empire" title="German Empire">Imperial German</a> naval force entered a village in Samoa, and in doing so destroyed some American property. Three American warships then entered the <a href="/wiki/Apia" title="Apia">Apia</a> harbor and prepared to engage the three German warships found there. Before any shots were fired, <a href="/wiki/1889_Apia_cyclone" title="1889 Apia cyclone">a typhoon</a> wrecked both the American and German ships. A compulsory <a href="/wiki/Armistice" title="Armistice">armistice</a> was then called because of the lack of any warships.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="20th_century">20th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: 20th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_20th_century">Early 20th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Early 20th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Elisala.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Elisala.jpg/170px-Elisala.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="233" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/30/Elisala.jpg/255px-Elisala.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/3/30/Elisala.jpg 2x" data-file-width="322" data-file-height="441" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Elisala.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Tuimanu&#x2bb;a Elisala Alalamua, the last official titleholder of <a href="/wiki/Tui_Manu%CA%BBa" class="mw-redirect" title="Tui Manuʻa">Tui Manuʻa</a> (1899–1909)</div></div></div>
<p>At the turn of the twentieth century, international rivalries in the latter half of the century were settled by the 1899 <a href="/wiki/Tripartite_Convention_(1899)" class="mw-redirect" title="Tripartite Convention (1899)">Tripartite Convention</a> in which Germany and the United States partitioned the <a href="/wiki/Samoan_Islands" title="Samoan Islands">Samoan Islands</a> into two:<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> the eastern island group became a territory of the United States (Tutuila in 1900 and officially Manuʻa in 1904)<sup id="cite_ref-DoI_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DoI-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> and is today known as American Samoa; the western islands, by far the greater landmass, became known as <a href="/wiki/German_Samoa" title="German Samoa">German Samoa</a>, after Britain gave up all claims to Samoa and in return accepted the termination of German rights in <a href="/wiki/Tonga" title="Tonga">Tonga</a> and certain areas in the <a href="/wiki/Solomon_Islands" title="Solomon Islands">Solomon Islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/West_Africa" title="West Africa">West Africa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Forerunners to the <a href="/wiki/Tripartite_Convention" title="Tripartite Convention">Tripartite Convention</a> of 1899 were the Washington Conference of 1887, the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Berlin_(1889)" title="Treaty of Berlin (1889)">Treaty of Berlin of 1889</a> and the Anglo-German Agreement on Samoa of 1899.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="American_colonization">American colonization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: American colonization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Benjamin_Franklin_Tilley_-_NH_67313.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Benjamin_Franklin_Tilley_-_NH_67313.jpg/170px-Benjamin_Franklin_Tilley_-_NH_67313.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="235" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Benjamin_Franklin_Tilley_-_NH_67313.jpg/255px-Benjamin_Franklin_Tilley_-_NH_67313.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Benjamin_Franklin_Tilley_-_NH_67313.jpg/340px-Benjamin_Franklin_Tilley_-_NH_67313.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4133" data-file-height="5715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Benjamin_Franklin_Tilley_-_NH_67313.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Rear_Admiral" class="mw-redirect" title="Rear Admiral">Rear Admiral</a> <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Franklin_Tilley" title="Benjamin Franklin Tilley">Benjamin Franklin Tilley</a>, the <a href="/wiki/List_of_governors_of_American_Samoa" title="List of governors of American Samoa">first</a> Governor of American Samoa (1900–1901)</div></div></div>
<p>The following year, the U.S. formally <a href="/wiki/Annexation" title="Annexation">annexed</a> its portion, a smaller group of eastern islands, one of which contains the noted harbor of <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago,_American_Samoa" class="mw-redirect" title="Pago Pago, American Samoa">Pago Pago</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Americans,_Almost_and_Forgotten_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Americans,_Almost_and_Forgotten-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> After the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Navy" title="United States Navy">United States Navy</a> took possession of eastern Samoa for the <a href="/wiki/Federal_government_of_the_United_States" title="Federal government of the United States">United States government</a>, the existing <a href="/wiki/Coaling_station" class="mw-redirect" title="Coaling station">coaling station</a> at Pago Pago Bay was expanded into a full <a href="/wiki/Naval_base" title="Naval base">naval station</a>, known as <a href="/wiki/United_States_Naval_Station_Tutuila" title="United States Naval Station Tutuila">United States Naval Station Tutuila</a> and commanded by a commandant. The Navy secured a <a href="/wiki/Deed_of_Cession_of_Tutuila" class="mw-redirect" title="Deed of Cession of Tutuila">Deed of Cession of Tutuila</a> in 1900 and a <a href="/wiki/Deed_of_Cession_of_Manu%CA%BBa" class="mw-redirect" title="Deed of Cession of Manuʻa">Deed of Cession of Manuʻa</a> in 1904 on behalf of the U.S. government. The last sovereign of Manuʻa, the <a href="/wiki/Tui_Manua_Elisala" class="mw-redirect" title="Tui Manua Elisala">Tui Manuʻa Elisala</a>, signed a Deed of Cession of Manuʻa following a series of U.S. naval trials, known as the "Trial of the Ipu", in Pago Pago, Taʻu, and aboard a <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Squadron" title="Pacific Squadron">Pacific Squadron</a> gunboat.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> The territory became known as the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Naval_Station_Tutuila" title="United States Naval Station Tutuila">U.S. Naval Station Tutuila</a>.
</p><p>On July 17, 1911, the U.S. Naval Station Tutuila, which was composed of Tutuila, <a href="/wiki/Aunu%CA%BBu" title="Aunuʻu">Aunuʻu</a> and Manuʻa, was officially renamed American Samoa.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> People of <a href="/wiki/Manu%CA%BBa" class="mw-redirect" title="Manuʻa">Manuʻa</a> had been unhappy since they were left out of the name "Naval Station Tutuila". In May 1911, Governor <a href="/wiki/William_Michael_Crose" title="William Michael Crose">William Michael Crose</a> authored a letter to the Secretary of the Navy conveying the sentiments of Manuʻa. The department responded that the people should choose a name for their new territory. The traditional leaders chose “American Samoa”, and, on July 7, 1911, the <a href="/wiki/Solicitor_General_of_the_Navy" title="Solicitor General of the Navy">solicitor general of the Navy</a> authorized the governor to proclaim it as the name for the new territory.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 209">&#58;&#8202;209&#8202;</span></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_I_and_the_1918_influenza_pandemic">World War I and the 1918 influenza pandemic</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: World War I and the 1918 influenza pandemic">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Gov12.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Gov12.JPG/170px-Gov12.JPG" decoding="async" width="170" height="216" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Gov12.JPG/255px-Gov12.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Gov12.JPG/340px-Gov12.JPG 2x" data-file-width="377" data-file-height="480" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Gov12.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/John_Martin_Poyer" title="John Martin Poyer">Commander John Martin Poyer</a> served as the <a href="/wiki/List_of_governors_of_American_Samoa" title="List of governors of American Samoa">12th</a> Governor of American Samoa (1915–1919).<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>In 1918, during the final stages of <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Spanish_flu" title="Spanish flu">Great Influenza epidemic</a> had taken its toll, spreading rapidly from country to country. American Samoa became one of the only places in the world (the others being <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia" title="New Caledonia">New Caledonia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Maraj%C3%B3" title="Marajó">Marajó island</a> in Brazil) to have proactively prevented any deaths during the pandemic through the quick response from <a href="/wiki/John_Martin_Poyer" title="John Martin Poyer">Governor John Martin Poyer</a> after hearing news reports of the outbreak on the radio and requesting <a href="/wiki/Quarantine_ships" class="mw-redirect" title="Quarantine ships">quarantine ships</a> from the U.S. mainland. The result of Poyer's quick actions earned him the <a href="/wiki/Navy_Cross" title="Navy Cross">Navy Cross</a> from the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Navy" title="United States Navy">U.S. Navy</a>. With this distinction, American Samoans regarded Poyer as their hero for what he had done to prevent the deadly disease. The neighboring New Zealand territory at the time, <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Western Samoa</a>, suffered the most of all <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Pacific Islands">Pacific islands</a>, with 90% of the population infected; 30% of adult men, 22% of adult women and 10% of children died.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Poyer offered assistance to help his New Zealand counterparts but was refused by the administrator of Western Samoa, <a href="/wiki/Robert_Logan_(politician)" title="Robert Logan (politician)">Robert Logan</a>, who became outraged after witnessing the number of quarantine ships surrounding American Samoa. Angered by this, Logan cut off communications with his American counterparts.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Interwar_period">Interwar period</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Interwar period">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="American_Samoa_Mau_movement">American Samoa Mau movement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: American Samoa Mau movement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
<p>After World War I, during the time of the <a href="/wiki/Mau_movement" title="Mau movement">Mau movement</a> in Western Samoa (then a <a href="/wiki/League_of_Nations" title="League of Nations">League of Nations</a> mandate governed by New Zealand), there was a corresponding American Samoa Mau movement led by Samuelu Ripley, a World War I veteran who was from <a href="/wiki/Leone,_American_Samoa" title="Leone, American Samoa">Leone</a> village, Tutuila. After meetings on the United States mainland, he was prevented from disembarking from the ship that brought him home to American Samoa and was not allowed to return because the American Samoa Mau movement was suppressed by the U.S. Navy. In 1930 the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Congress" title="United States Congress">U.S. Congress</a> sent a committee to investigate the status of American Samoa, led by Americans who had a part in the overthrow of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hawaii" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Hawaii">Kingdom of Hawaii</a>.
</p>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Annexation_of_Swains_Island">Annexation of Swains Island</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Annexation of Swains Island">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
<p><a href="/wiki/Swains_Island" title="Swains Island">Swains Island</a>, which had been included in the list of guano islands <a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/appertain" class="extiw" title="wikt:appertain">appertaining</a> to the United States and bonded under the <a href="/wiki/Guano_Islands_Act" title="Guano Islands Act">Guano Islands Act</a>, was <a href="/wiki/Annexation" title="Annexation">annexed</a> in 1925 by Pub. Res. 68–75,<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> following the dissolution of the <a href="/wiki/Gilbert_and_Ellice_Islands#Gilbert_and_Ellice_Islands_Colony_(GEIC)" title="Gilbert and Ellice Islands">Gilbert and Ellice Islands Colony</a> by the United Kingdom.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_II_and_aftermath">World War II and aftermath</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: World War II and aftermath">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>During <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, <a href="/wiki/United_States_Marine_Corps" title="United States Marine Corps">U.S. Marines</a> stationed in Samoa outnumbered the local population and had a huge cultural influence. Young Samoan men from age 14 and above were combat trained by <a href="/wiki/United_States_Armed_Forces" title="United States Armed Forces">U.S. military personnel</a>. Samoans served in various capacities during World War II, including as combatants, medical personnel, code personnel, and ship repairmen.
</p><p>In 1949, Organic Act 4500, a <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_the_Interior" title="United States Department of the Interior">U.S. Department of Interior</a>–sponsored attempt to incorporate American Samoa, was introduced in Congress. It was ultimately defeated, primarily through the efforts of Samoan chiefs, led by Tuiasosopo Mariota.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The efforts of these chiefs led to the creation of a territorial legislature, the <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_Fono" title="American Samoa Fono">American Samoa Fono</a>, which meets in the village of <a href="/wiki/Fagatogo" title="Fagatogo">Fagatogo</a>. In 1950 the Department of the Interior began to administer the American Samoa.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="1951.E2.80.931999"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="1951–1999">1951–1999</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: 1951–1999">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:332px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Splashdown_2.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Splashdown_2.png/330px-Splashdown_2.png" decoding="async" width="330" height="201" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fa/Splashdown_2.png/495px-Splashdown_2.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/fa/Splashdown_2.png 2x" data-file-width="657" data-file-height="400" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Splashdown_2.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Locations of <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ocean" title="Pacific Ocean">Pacific Ocean</a> <a href="/wiki/Splashdown" title="Splashdown">splashdowns</a> of American spacecraft</div></div></div>
<p>By 1956, the U.S. Navy-appointed governor was replaced by <a href="/wiki/Peter_Tali_Coleman" title="Peter Tali Coleman">Peter Tali Coleman</a>, who was locally elected. Although technically considered "unorganized" since the U.S. Congress has not passed an <a href="/wiki/Organic_Act" class="mw-redirect" title="Organic Act">Organic Act</a> for the territory, American Samoa is self-governing under a <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_American_Samoa" title="Constitution of American Samoa">constitution</a> that became effective on July 1, 1967. The U.S. Territory of American Samoa is on the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_list_of_non-self-governing_territories" title="United Nations list of non-self-governing territories">United Nations list of non-self-governing territories</a>, a listing which is disputed by the territorial government officials, who do consider themselves to be self-governing.
</p><p>American Samoa and <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago_International_Airport" title="Pago Pago International Airport">Pago Pago International Airport</a> had historic significance with the <a href="/wiki/Apollo_Program" class="mw-redirect" title="Apollo Program">Apollo Program</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Astronaut" title="Astronaut">astronaut</a> crews of <a href="/wiki/Apollo_10" title="Apollo 10">Apollo 10</a>, <a href="/wiki/Apollo_12" title="Apollo 12">12</a>, <a href="/wiki/Apollo_13" title="Apollo 13">13</a>, <a href="/wiki/Apollo_14" title="Apollo 14">14</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Apollo_17" title="Apollo 17">17</a> were retrieved a few hundred miles from Pago Pago and transported by helicopter to the airport prior to being flown to Honolulu on <a href="/wiki/C-141_Starlifter" class="mw-redirect" title="C-141 Starlifter">C-141 Starlifter</a> military aircraft.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While the two Samoas share language and ethnicity, their cultures have recently followed different paths, with American Samoans often emigrating to <a href="/wiki/Hawaii" title="Hawaii">Hawai&#x2bb;i</a> and the U.S. mainland, and adopting many U.S. customs, such as the playing of <a href="/wiki/American_football" title="American football">American football</a> and <a href="/wiki/Baseball" title="Baseball">baseball</a>. <a href="/wiki/Samoans" title="Samoans">Samoans</a> have tended to emigrate instead to <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a>, whose influence has made the sports of <a href="/wiki/Rugby_football" title="Rugby football">rugby</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cricket" title="Cricket">cricket</a> more popular in the western Samoan islands. Travel writer <a href="/wiki/Paul_Theroux" title="Paul Theroux">Paul Theroux</a> noted that there were marked differences between the societies in <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a> and American Samoa.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="21st_century">21st century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: 21st century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>American Samoans have a high rate of service in the <a href="/wiki/U.S._Armed_Forces" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. Armed Forces">U.S. Armed Forces</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Because of economic hardship, military service has been seen as an opportunity in American Samoa and other <a href="/wiki/Territories_of_the_United_States#Classification_of_current_U.S._territories" title="Territories of the United States">U.S. Overseas territories</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notable_events">Notable events</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Notable events">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Pre-20th_century">Pre-20th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Pre-20th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Death_of_Langle.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Death_of_Langle.png/220px-Death_of_Langle.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Death_of_Langle.png/330px-Death_of_Langle.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Death_of_Langle.png/440px-Death_of_Langle.png 2x" data-file-width="866" data-file-height="588" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Death_of_Langle.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Death of <a href="/wiki/Paul_Antoine_Fleuriot_de_Langle" title="Paul Antoine Fleuriot de Langle">Fleuriot de Langle</a> in 1787</div></div></div>
<p>On December 13, 1784, French navigator <a href="/wiki/Jean-Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Galaup,_comte_de_Lap%C3%A9rouse" title="Jean-François de Galaup, comte de Lapérouse">Jean-François de Galaup, comte de Lapérouse</a> landed two exploration parties on Tutuila's north shore: one from the ship <i><a href="/wiki/French_ship_Boussole_(1781)" title="French ship Boussole (1781)">La Boussole</a></i> at Fagasa, and the other from <i><a href="/wiki/French_ship_Astrolabe_(1781)" title="French ship Astrolabe (1781)">L'Astrolabe</a></i> at <a href="/wiki/A%27asu,_American_Samoa" title="A&#39;asu, American Samoa">Aʻasu</a>. One of the cooks, David, died of "scorbutic dropsy". On December 11, twelve members of Lapérouse's crew (including First Officer <a href="/wiki/Paul_Antoine_Fleuriot_de_Langle" title="Paul Antoine Fleuriot de Langle">Paul Antoine Fleuriot de Langle</a>) were killed by angry Samoans at Aʻasu Bay, Tutuila, thereafter known as "Massacre Bay", which Lapérouse described as "this den, more fearful from its treacherous situation and the cruelty of its inhabitants than the lair of a lion or a tiger". This incident gave Samoa a reputation for savagery that kept Europeans away until the arrival of the first Christian missionaries four decades later. On December 12, at Aʻasu Bay, Lapérouse ordered his gunners to fire one cannonball amid the attackers who had killed his men the day before and were now returning to launch another attack. He later wrote in his journal "I could have destroyed or sunk a hundred canoes, with more than 500 people in them: but I was afraid of striking the wrong victims; the call of my conscience saved their lives."<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="20th_century_2">20th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: 20th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:SADIE_THOMPSON_BUILDING.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/SADIE_THOMPSON_BUILDING.jpg/220px-SADIE_THOMPSON_BUILDING.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/SADIE_THOMPSON_BUILDING.jpg/330px-SADIE_THOMPSON_BUILDING.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/88/SADIE_THOMPSON_BUILDING.jpg/440px-SADIE_THOMPSON_BUILDING.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3000" data-file-height="1993" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:SADIE_THOMPSON_BUILDING.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>English author <a href="/wiki/W._Somerset_Maugham" title="W. Somerset Maugham">W. Somerset Maugham</a> stayed at <a href="/wiki/Sadie_Thompson_Inn" title="Sadie Thompson Inn">Sadie Thompson Inn</a> during his six-week visit to <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago" title="Pago Pago">Pago Pago</a> in 1916.</div></div></div>
<p>On December 19, 1912, English writer <a href="/wiki/William_Somerset_Maugham" class="mw-redirect" title="William Somerset Maugham">William Somerset Maugham</a> arrived in Pago Pago, allegedly accompanied by a missionary and Miss Sadie Thompson. His visit inspired his short story "<a href="/wiki/Rain_(short_story)" title="Rain (short story)">Rain</a>" which later became plays and three major motion pictures. The building still stands where Maugham stayed and has been renamed the <a href="/wiki/Sadie_Thompson_Building" class="mw-redirect" title="Sadie Thompson Building">Sadie Thompson Building</a>. Today it is a prominent restaurant and inn.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On November 2, 1921, American Samoa's 13th <a href="/wiki/List_of_governors_of_American_Samoa" title="List of governors of American Samoa">naval governor</a>, Commander <a href="/wiki/Warren_Jay_Terhune" class="mw-redirect" title="Warren Jay Terhune">Warren Jay Terhune</a>, died by suicide with a pistol in the bathroom of the government mansion, overlooking the entrance to Pago Pago Harbor. His body was discovered by Government House's cook, SDI<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The text near this tag may need clarification or removal of jargon. (November 2017)">clarification needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> First Class Felisiano Debid Ahchica, USN. His ghost is rumored to walk about the grounds at night.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Fagatogo_Dock.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Fagatogo_Dock.jpg/220px-Fagatogo_Dock.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="158" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Fagatogo_Dock.jpg/330px-Fagatogo_Dock.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Fagatogo_Dock.jpg/440px-Fagatogo_Dock.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1571" data-file-height="1131" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Fagatogo_Dock.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pago Pago Harbor today and inter-island dock area</div></div></div>
<p>On August 17, 1924, <a href="/wiki/Margaret_Mead" title="Margaret Mead">Margaret Mead</a> arrived in American Samoa aboard the SS <i>Sonoma</i> to begin fieldwork for her doctoral dissertation in anthropology at Columbia University, where she was a student of Professor <a href="/wiki/Franz_Boas" title="Franz Boas">Franz Boas</a>. Her work <i><a href="/wiki/Coming_of_Age_in_Samoa" title="Coming of Age in Samoa">Coming of Age in Samoa</a></i> was published in 1928, at the time becoming the most widely read book in the field of <a href="/wiki/Anthropology" title="Anthropology">anthropology</a>. The book has sparked years of ongoing and intense debate and controversy. Mead returned to American Samoa in 1971 for the dedication of the <a href="/wiki/Jean_P._Haydon_Museum" title="Jean P. Haydon Museum">Jean P. Haydon Museum</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sikorsky_S42_(crop).jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Sikorsky_S42_%28crop%29.jpg/220px-Sikorsky_S42_%28crop%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="171" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Sikorsky_S42_%28crop%29.jpg/330px-Sikorsky_S42_%28crop%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Sikorsky_S42_%28crop%29.jpg/440px-Sikorsky_S42_%28crop%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="531" data-file-height="412" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sikorsky_S42_(crop).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <i>Samoan Clipper</i></div></div></div>
<p>In 1938, the noted aviator <a href="/wiki/Ed_Musick" title="Ed Musick">Ed Musick</a> and his crew died on the <a href="/wiki/Pan_American_World_Airways" class="mw-redirect" title="Pan American World Airways">Pan American World Airways</a> S-42 <i><a href="/wiki/Samoan_Clipper" title="Samoan Clipper">Samoan Clipper</a></i> over Pago Pago, while on a survey flight to <a href="/wiki/Auckland" title="Auckland">Auckland</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a>. Sometime after takeoff, the aircraft experienced trouble, and Musick turned it back toward Pago Pago. While the crew dumped fuel in preparation for an emergency landing, an explosion occurred that tore the aircraft apart.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On November 21, 1939, American Samoa's last execution was carried out. Imoa was convicted of stabbing Sema to death and was <a href="/wiki/Hanging" title="Hanging">hanged</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Custom_house" title="Custom house">Customs House</a>. The popular Samoan song "Faʻafofoga Samoa" is based on this, said to be the final words of Imoa.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On January 13, 1942, at 2:26<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>am, a Japanese submarine surfaced off Tutuila between Southworth Point and <a href="/wiki/Fagasa,_American_Samoa" class="mw-redirect" title="Fagasa, American Samoa">Fagasa</a> Bay and fired about 15 shells from its 5.5-inch deck gun at the U.S. Naval Station Tutuila over the next 10 minutes. The first shell struck the rear of Frank Shimasaki's store, ironically owned by one of Tutuila's few Japanese residents. The store was closed, as Mr. Shimasaki had been interned as an enemy alien. The next shell caused slight damage to the naval dispensary, the third landed on the lawn behind the naval quarters known as "Centipede Row," and the fourth struck the stone seawall outside the customs house. The other rounds fell harmlessly into the harbor. As one writer described it, "The fire was not returned, notwithstanding the eagerness of the Samoan Marines to test their skill against the enemy<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>... No American or Samoan Marines were wounded."<sup id="cite_ref-Enright_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Enright-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Commander Edwin B. Robinson was bicycling behind Centipede Row and was wounded in the knee by a piece of <a href="/wiki/Shrapnel_(fragment)" class="mw-redirect" title="Shrapnel (fragment)">shrapnel</a>, and "a member of the colorful native Fita Fita Guard" received minor injuries; they were the only casualties. This was the only time the Japanese attacked Tutuila during <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, although "Japanese submarines had patrolled the waters around Samoa before the war, and continued to be active there throughout the war."<sup id="cite_ref-Enright_37-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Enright-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On August 24, 1943, First Lady <a href="/wiki/Eleanor_Roosevelt" title="Eleanor Roosevelt">Eleanor Roosevelt</a> visited American Samoa and inspected the Fita Fita Guard and Band and the First Samoan Battalion of U.S. Marine Corps Reserve at the U.S. Naval Station American Samoa.<sup id="cite_ref-Shaffer-2000_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Shaffer-2000-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 178">&#58;&#8202;178&#8202;</span></sup><sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> The fact that First Lady reviewed the troops led to further assurance that Tutuila Island was considered safe.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Her presence underscored that World War II had passed by American Samoa. While the Fita Fita band played, Eleanor Roosevelt inspected the guard.<sup id="cite_ref-Ruck_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ruck-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On October 18, 1966, President <a href="/wiki/Lyndon_Baines_Johnson" class="mw-redirect" title="Lyndon Baines Johnson">Lyndon Baines Johnson</a> and First Lady <a href="/wiki/Lady_Bird_Johnson" title="Lady Bird Johnson">Lady Bird Johnson</a> visited American Samoa. Mrs. Johnson dedicated the "Manulele Tausala" ("Lady Bird") Elementary School in <a href="/wiki/Nu%27uuli,_American_Samoa" title="Nu&#39;uuli, American Samoa">Nuʻuuli</a>, which was named after her. Johnson is the only US president to have visited American Samoa, while Mrs. Johnson was the second First Lady, preceded by Eleanor Roosevelt in 1943.<sup id="cite_ref-Shaffer-2000_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Shaffer-2000-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 192">&#58;&#8202;192&#8202;</span></sup> The territory's only hospital was renamed the <a href="/wiki/LBJ_Tropical_Medical_Center" title="LBJ Tropical Medical Center">LBJ Tropical Medical Center</a> in honor of President Johnson.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the late 1960s and early 1970s, American Samoa played a pivotal role in five of the <a href="/wiki/Apollo_program" title="Apollo program">Apollo Program missions</a>. The astronauts landed several hundred miles from Pago and were transported to the islands en route back to the mainland. President <a href="/wiki/Richard_Nixon" title="Richard Nixon">Richard Nixon</a> gave three moon rocks to the American Samoan government, and these are on display in the Jean P. Haydon Museum, along with a flag carried to the moon on one of the missions.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In November 1970, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Paul_VI" title="Pope Paul VI">Pope Paul VI</a> visited American Samoa in a brief but lavish greeting.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 292">&#58;&#8202;292&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>On January 30, 1974, <a href="/wiki/Pan_Am_Flight_806" title="Pan Am Flight 806">Pan Am Flight 806</a> from <a href="/wiki/Auckland" title="Auckland">Auckland</a>, New Zealand crashed at Pago Pago International Airport at 10:41<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>pm, with 91 passengers aboard. 86 people were killed, including Captain Leroy A. Petersen and the entire flight crew. Four of the five surviving passengers were seriously injured, with the other only slightly injured. The airliner was destroyed by the impact and succeeding fire. The crash was attributed to poor visibility, pilot error, or wind shear since a violent storm was raging at the time.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> In January 2014, filmmaker Paul Crompton visited the territory to interview local residents for a documentary film about the 1974 crash.
</p><p>A U.S. Navy <a href="/wiki/P-3_Orion" class="mw-redirect" title="P-3 Orion">P-3 Orion</a> patrol plane from <a href="/w/index.php?title=Patrol_Squadron_50&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Patrol Squadron 50 (page does not exist)">Patrol Squadron 50</a> (VP-50) had its vertical stabilizer shorn off by the Solo Ridge-Mount Alava <a href="/wiki/Aerial_tramway" title="Aerial tramway">aerial tramway</a> cable across Pago Pago harbor on April 17, 1980, during the <a href="/wiki/Flag_Day_(United_States)" title="Flag Day (United States)">Flag Day</a> celebrations, when carrying six skydivers from the U.S. Army's Hawaii-based Tropic Lightning Parachute Club. The plane crashed, demolishing a wing of the <a href="/wiki/Rainmaker_Hotel" title="Rainmaker Hotel">Rainmaker Hotel</a> and killing all six crew members and one civilian. The six skydivers had already left the aircraft during a demonstration jump. A memorial monument is erected on Mt. Mauga O Aliʻi to honor their memory.
</p><p>On November 1, 1988, President <a href="/wiki/Ronald_Reagan" title="Ronald Reagan">Ronald Reagan</a> signed a bill which created <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_National_Park" class="mw-redirect" title="American Samoa National Park">American Samoa National Park</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="21st_century_2">21st century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: 21st century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>On July 22, 2010, Detective Lieutenant Lusila Brown was fatally shot outside the temporary High Court building in Fagatogo. It was the first time in more than 15 years that a police officer was killed in the line of duty. The last was Sa Fuimaono, who drowned after saving a teenager from rough seas.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On November 8, 2010, <a href="/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_State" title="United States Secretary of State">United States Secretary of State</a> and former <a href="/wiki/First_Lady_of_the_United_States" title="First Lady of the United States">First Lady</a> <a href="/wiki/Hillary_Clinton" title="Hillary Clinton">Hillary Clinton</a> made a refueling stopover at the <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago_International_Airport" title="Pago Pago International Airport">Pago Pago International Airport</a>. She was greeted by government dignitaries and presented with gifts and a traditional ava ceremony.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Mike_Pence" title="Mike Pence">Mike Pence</a> was the third sitting U.S. vice president to visit American Samoa (after <a href="/wiki/Dan_Quayle" title="Dan Quayle">Dan Quayle</a> and <a href="/wiki/Joe_Biden" title="Joe Biden">Joe Biden</a>)<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> when he made a stopover in Pago Pago in April 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> He addressed 200 soldiers here during his refueling stop.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> U.S. Secretary of State <a href="/wiki/Rex_Tillerson" title="Rex Tillerson">Rex Tillerson</a> visited town on June 3, 2017.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="September_2009_earthquake_and_tsunami">September 2009 earthquake and tsunami</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: September 2009 earthquake and tsunami">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kermadec_Arc.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Kermadec_Arc.jpg/220px-Kermadec_Arc.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="337" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Kermadec_Arc.jpg/330px-Kermadec_Arc.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/Kermadec_Arc.jpg/440px-Kermadec_Arc.jpg 2x" data-file-width="585" data-file-height="897" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kermadec_Arc.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Tonga_Trench" title="Tonga Trench">Tonga Trench</a> south of the Samoa Islands and north of <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a></div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/2009_Samoa_earthquake_and_tsunami" title="2009 Samoa earthquake and tsunami">2009 Samoa earthquake and tsunami</a></div>
<p>On September 28, 2009, at 17:48:11 UTC, an 8.1 <a href="/wiki/Richter_magnitude_scale" title="Richter magnitude scale">magnitude</a> <a href="/wiki/Earthquake" title="Earthquake">earthquake</a> struck 120 miles (190&#160;km) off the coast of American Samoa, followed by smaller aftershocks.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> It was the <a href="/wiki/List_of_21st-century_earthquakes#2009" class="mw-redirect" title="List of 21st-century earthquakes">largest</a> earthquake of 2009. The quake occurred on the <a href="/wiki/Outer_trench_swell" title="Outer trench swell">outer rise</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Kermadec-Tonga_Subduction_Zone" class="mw-redirect" title="Kermadec-Tonga Subduction Zone">Kermadec-Tonga Subduction Zone</a>. This is part of the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ring_of_Fire" class="mw-redirect" title="Pacific Ring of Fire">Pacific Ring of Fire</a>, where <a href="/wiki/Plate_tectonics" title="Plate tectonics">tectonic plates</a> in the Earth's <a href="/wiki/Lithosphere" title="Lithosphere">lithosphere</a> meet, and earthquakes and volcanic activity are common. The quake struck 11.2 miles (18.0&#160;km) below the ocean floor and generated an onsetting <a href="/wiki/Tsunami" title="Tsunami">tsunami</a> that killed more than 170 people in the Samoa Islands and <a href="/wiki/Tonga" title="Tonga">Tonga</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> Four waves with heights from 15 feet (4.6&#160;m) to 20 feet (6.1&#160;m) high were reported to have reached up to one mile (1.6<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>km) inland on the island of Tutuila.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Defense_Logistics_Agency" title="Defense Logistics Agency">Defense Logistics Agency</a> worked with the <a href="/wiki/Federal_Emergency_Management_Agency" title="Federal Emergency Management Agency">Federal Emergency Management Agency</a> to provide 16' × 16' humanitarian tents to the devastated areas of American Samoa.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Government and politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Government">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Government_of_American_Samoa" title="Government of American Samoa">Government of American Samoa</a></div>
<p>American Samoa is classified in U.S. law as an <a href="/wiki/Unincorporated_territories_of_the_United_States" class="mw-redirect" title="Unincorporated territories of the United States">unincorporated territory</a>; the <a href="/wiki/Ratification_Act_of_1929" title="Ratification Act of 1929">Ratification Act of 1929</a> vested all civil, judicial, and military powers in the <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_United_States" title="President of the United States">President of the United States</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Americans,_Almost_and_Forgotten_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Americans,_Almost_and_Forgotten-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> In 1951, with <a href="/wiki/Executive_order" title="Executive order">Executive Order</a> <a href="https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Executive_Order_10264" class="extiw" title="s:Executive Order 10264">10264</a>, President Harry Truman delegated that authority to the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_the_Interior" title="United States Secretary of the Interior">Secretary of the Interior</a>. On June 21, 1963 <a href="/wiki/Tuli_Le%CA%BBiato" title="Tuli Leʻiato">Paramount Chief Tuli Leʻiato of Fagaʻitua</a> was sworn in and installed as the first Secretary of Samoan Affairs by Governor <a href="/wiki/H._Rex_Lee" title="H. Rex Lee">H. Rex Lee</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> On June 2, 1967, Interior Secretary <a href="/wiki/Stewart_Udall" title="Stewart Udall">Stewart Udall</a> promulgated the Revised Constitution of American Samoa, which took effect on July 1, 1967.<sup id="cite_ref-constitution_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitution-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Lemanu_Peleti_Mauga.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Lemanu_Peleti_Mauga.jpg/220px-Lemanu_Peleti_Mauga.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="200" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Lemanu_Peleti_Mauga.jpg/330px-Lemanu_Peleti_Mauga.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0b/Lemanu_Peleti_Mauga.jpg/440px-Lemanu_Peleti_Mauga.jpg 2x" data-file-width="944" data-file-height="860" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Lemanu_Peleti_Mauga.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Lemanu_Peleti_Mauga" title="Lemanu Peleti Mauga">Lemanu Peleti Mauga</a>, the <a href="/wiki/List_of_governors_of_American_Samoa" title="List of governors of American Samoa">58th</a> and incumbent Governor of American Samoa (2021–present)</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_American_Samoa" class="mw-redirect" title="Governor of American Samoa">Governor of American Samoa</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a> and along with the <a href="/wiki/Lieutenant_Governor_of_American_Samoa" title="Lieutenant Governor of American Samoa">Lieutenant Governor of American Samoa</a> is elected on the same <a href="/wiki/Ticket_(election)" title="Ticket (election)">ticket</a> by <a href="/wiki/Direct_election" title="Direct election">popular vote</a> for a four-year term.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> The governor's office is located in <a href="/wiki/Utulei,_American_Samoa" title="Utulei, American Samoa">Utulei</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-executive_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-executive-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-npsmap_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-npsmap-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Since American Samoa is a U.S. territory, the President of the United States serves as the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> but does not play a direct role in government. The Secretary of the Interior oversees the government, retaining the power to approve constitutional amendments, overrides the governor's <a href="/wiki/Veto" title="Veto">vetoes</a>, and nomination of justices.<sup id="cite_ref-constitution_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitution-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Legislative_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative power">legislative power</a> is vested in the <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_Fono" title="American Samoa Fono">American Samoa Fono</a>, which has <a href="/wiki/Bicameralism" title="Bicameralism">two chambers</a>. The <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_House_of_Representatives" title="American Samoa House of Representatives">House of Representatives</a> has 21 members serving two-year terms, being 20 representatives popularly elected from various districts and one non-voting delegate from <a href="/wiki/Swains_Island" title="Swains Island">Swains Island</a> elected in a public meeting. The <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_Senate" title="American Samoa Senate">Senate</a> has 18 members, elected for four-year terms by and from the <a href="/wiki/Fa%CA%BBamatai" title="Faʻamatai">chiefs of the islands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-constitution_57-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitution-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> The Fono is located in <a href="/wiki/Fagatogo" title="Fagatogo">Fagatogo</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-noaa_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-noaa-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-npsmap_60-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-npsmap-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Judiciary_of_American_Samoa" title="Judiciary of American Samoa">judiciary of American Samoa</a> is composed of the <a href="/wiki/High_Court_of_American_Samoa" title="High Court of American Samoa">High Court of American Samoa</a>, a District Court, and village courts.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> The High Court and District Court are located in Fagatogo, near the Fono.<sup id="cite_ref-highcourt_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-highcourt-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-districtcourt_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-districtcourt-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-noaa_61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-noaa-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> The High Court is led by a Chief Justice and an Associate Justice, appointed by the Secretary of the Interior.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> Other judges are appointed by the governor upon the recommendation of the Chief Justice and confirmed by the Senate.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_American_Samoa" title="Politics of American Samoa">Politics of American Samoa</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Elections_in_American_Samoa" title="Elections in American Samoa">Elections in American Samoa</a> and <a href="/wiki/Political_party_strength_in_American_Samoa" title="Political party strength in American Samoa">Political party strength in American Samoa</a></div> 
<p>American Samoa is an unincorporated and <a href="/wiki/Territories_of_the_United_States" title="Territories of the United States">unorganized territory</a> of the United States, administered by the <a href="/wiki/Office_of_Insular_Affairs" title="Office of Insular Affairs">Office of Insular Affairs</a>, <a href="/wiki/U.S._Department_of_the_Interior" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. Department of the Interior">U.S. Department of the Interior</a>. American Samoa's constitution was ratified in 1966 and came into effect in 1967. 
</p><p>However, despite being <a href="/wiki/De_jure" title="De jure">de jure</a> unorganized, American Samoa is <a href="/wiki/De_facto" title="De facto">de facto</a> organized, with its politics taking place in the framework of a <a href="/wiki/Presidential_system" title="Presidential system">presidential</a> <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representative democratic</a> <a href="/wiki/Dependent_territory" title="Dependent territory">dependency</a>, whereby the <a href="/wiki/List_of_American_Samoa_Governors" class="mw-redirect" title="List of American Samoa Governors">Governor</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>, and of a <a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/pluriform" class="extiw" title="wikt:pluriform">pluriform</a> <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party system</a>. 
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Executive_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Executive power">Executive power</a> is exercised by the governor. <a href="/wiki/Legislative_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative power">Legislative power</a> is vested in the two chambers of the legislature. The <a href="/wiki/Political_parties_in_the_United_States" title="Political parties in the United States">American political parties</a> (<a href="/wiki/Republican_Party_(United_States)" title="Republican Party (United States)">Republican</a> and <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_(United_States)" title="Democratic Party (United States)">Democratic</a>) exist in American Samoa, but few politicians are aligned with the parties. The <a href="/wiki/Judiciary" title="Judiciary">judiciary</a> is independent of the <a href="/wiki/Executive_(government)" title="Executive (government)">executive</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Legislature" title="Legislature">legislature</a>. 
</p><p>There is also the traditional village politics of the Samoa Islands, the "<a href="/wiki/Fa%CA%BBamatai" title="Faʻamatai">faʻamatai</a>" and the "<a href="/wiki/Fa%CA%BBa_S%C4%81moa" title="Faʻa Sāmoa">faʻa Sāmoa</a>", which continues in American Samoa and independent Samoa, and which interacts across these current boundaries. The faʻa Sāmoa is the language and customs, and the faʻamatai are the protocols of the "fono" (council) and the chief system. The faʻamatai and the fono take place at all levels of the Samoan body politic, from the family to the village, to the region, to national matters.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/%CA%BBAiga" title="ʻAiga">ʻaiga</a> is the family unit of Samoan society, which differs from the Western sense of a family<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> in that it consists of an "extended family" based on the culture's communal <a href="/wiki/Political_sociology" title="Political sociology">socio-political</a> organization. The head of the ʻaiga is the matai. The matai (chiefs) are elected by consensus within the fono of the extended family and village(s) concerned. The matai and the fono, which are themselves made of matai, decide on the distribution of family exchanges and tenancy of communal lands. The majority of lands in American Samoa and independent Samoa are <a href="/wiki/Agricultural_commune" title="Agricultural commune">communal</a>. A matai can represent a small family group or a great extended family that reaches across islands and to both American Samoa and independent Samoa.
</p><p>In 2010, voters rejected a <a href="/wiki/American_Samoan_constitutional_referendum,_2010" class="mw-redirect" title="American Samoan constitutional referendum, 2010">package of amendments</a> to the territorial constitution, which would have, among other things, allowed U.S. citizens to be legislators only if they had Samoan ancestry.
</p><p>In 2012, both the Governor and American Samoa's delegate to the U.S. Congress <a href="/wiki/Eni_Faleomavaega" title="Eni Faleomavaega">Eni Faleomavaega</a> called for the populace to consider a move towards autonomy if not independence, with a mixed response.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Nationality">Nationality</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Nationality">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Tuaua_v._United_States" title="Tuaua v. United States">Tuaua v. United States</a></div>
<p>According to the <a href="/wiki/Immigration_and_Nationality_Act" title="Immigration and Nationality Act">Immigration and Nationality Act</a> (INA), the people born in American Samoa—including those born on <a href="/wiki/Swains_Island" title="Swains Island">Swains Island</a>—are "<a href="/wiki/National_of_the_United_States" class="mw-redirect" title="National of the United States">nationals</a> but not <a href="/wiki/Citizenship_of_the_United_States" title="Citizenship of the United States">citizens of the United States</a> at birth".<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> If a child is born on any of these islands to any U.S. citizen, then that child is considered a national and a citizen of the United States at birth.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> All U.S. nationals have statutory rights to reside in all parts of the United States, and may apply for citizenship by <a href="/wiki/Naturalization" title="Naturalization">naturalization</a> after three months of residency by paying a fee, passing a test in English and civics, and taking an oath of allegiance to the United States.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> All U.S. nationals also have the right to work in the United States, except in certain government jobs that specifically require U.S. citizenship.
</p><p>In 2012, a group of American Samoans sued the federal government seeking recognition of birthright citizenship for American Samoans in the case <i><a href="/wiki/Tuaua_v._United_States" title="Tuaua v. United States">Tuaua v. United States</a></i>. In an <a href="/wiki/Amicus_curiae" title="Amicus curiae">amicus curiae</a> brief filed in federal court, American Samoan Congressman Faleomavaega supported the legal interpretation that the <a href="/wiki/Citizenship_Clause" title="Citizenship Clause">Citizenship Clause</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Fourteenth_Amendment_to_the_United_States_Constitution" title="Fourteenth Amendment to the United States Constitution">Fourteenth Amendment</a> does not extend <a href="/wiki/Birthright_citizenship_in_the_United_States" title="Birthright citizenship in the United States">birthright citizenship</a> to United States nationals born in unincorporated territories.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> In June 2015, the <a href="/wiki/U.S._Court_of_Appeals_for_the_District_of_Columbia" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. Court of Appeals for the District of Columbia">U.S. Court of Appeals for the District of Columbia</a> affirmed that Fourteenth Amendment citizenship guarantees did not apply to persons born in unincorporated territories and a year later the U.S. Supreme Court declined to review the lower court's decision.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In December 2019, U.S. District Judge <a href="/wiki/Clark_Waddoups" title="Clark Waddoups">Clark Waddoups</a> struck down <span class="plainlinksneverexpand"><a href="/wiki/Title_8_of_the_United_States_Code" title="Title 8 of the United States Code">8 U.S.C.</a>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/8/1408#1">§&#160;1408(1)</a></span> as facially unconstitutional, holding that "Persons born in American Samoa are citizens of the United States by the Citizenship Clause of the Fourteenth Amendment",<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> but the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Court_of_Appeals_for_the_Tenth_Circuit" title="United States Court of Appeals for the Tenth Circuit">United States Court of Appeals for the Tenth Circuit</a> reversed the district court's judgment and found the statute constitutional.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> On July 20, 2021, the Legislature of American Samoa unanimously passed a resolution in support of the 10th Circuit Court's decision to reverse.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h5><span class="mw-headline" id="Voting_rights">Voting rights</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Voting rights">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h5>
