<p>As U.S. nationals, American Samoans can vote in local elections in the territory; however, if they live in other parts of the United States, they are <a href="/wiki/Right_of_foreigners_to_vote_in_the_United_States" class="mw-redirect" title="Right of foreigners to vote in the United States">not allowed to vote</a> in federal, state or the vast majority of local elections unless they become U.S. citizens. The only federal office American Samoans elect directly is a <a href="/wiki/Delegate_(United_States_Congress)" class="mw-redirect" title="Delegate (United States Congress)">non-voting delegate</a> to the <a href="/wiki/United_States_House_of_Representatives" title="United States House of Representatives">United States House of Representatives</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> Since the <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa%27s_at-large_congressional_district" title="American Samoa&#39;s at-large congressional district">delegate's office</a> was created in 1978, three people have held the seat: <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Party_(United_States)" title="Democratic Party (United States)">Democrat</a> <a href="/wiki/Fof%C5%8D_Iosefa_Fiti_Sunia" title="Fofō Iosefa Fiti Sunia">Fofō Iosefa Fiti Sunia</a> (1981–1988); Democrat <a href="/wiki/Eni_Faleomavaega" title="Eni Faleomavaega">Eni Faleomavaega</a> (1989–2015); and <a href="/wiki/Republican_Party_(United_States)" title="Republican Party (United States)">Republican</a> <a href="/wiki/Aumua_Amata_Radewagen" class="mw-redirect" title="Aumua Amata Radewagen">Aumua Amata Radewagen</a> (2015–)<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> American Samoans also participate in partisan <a href="/wiki/United_States_presidential_primary" title="United States presidential primary">presidential primaries</a>, as well as send delegates to the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_National_Convention" title="Democratic National Convention">Democratic</a> and <a href="/wiki/Republican_National_Convention" title="Republican National Convention">Republican</a> National Conventions.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Immigration">Immigration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Immigration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Unique among U.S. territories, American Samoa has its own immigration law, separate from the <a href="/wiki/List_of_United_States_immigration_laws" title="List of United States immigration laws">laws that apply</a> in other parts of the United States. U.S. nationals may freely reside in American Samoa.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;c&#93;</a></sup> The American Samoan government, via its Immigration Office, controls the migration of foreign nationals to the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Special application forms exist for migration to American Samoa based on family or employment sponsorship.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Unlike all other permanently inhabited U.S. jurisdictions (<a href="/wiki/U.S._state" title="U.S. state">states</a>, <a href="/wiki/District_of_Columbia" class="mw-redirect" title="District of Columbia">District of Columbia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Puerto_Rico" title="Puerto Rico">Puerto Rico</a>, <a href="/wiki/U.S._Virgin_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. Virgin Islands">U.S. Virgin Islands</a>, <a href="/wiki/Guam" title="Guam">Guam</a> and <a href="/wiki/Northern_Mariana_Islands" title="Northern Mariana Islands">Northern Mariana Islands</a>), American Samoa is not considered a U.S. state for the purposes of the U.S. Immigration and Nationality Act.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> As a result, there is no path for immigrants to American Samoa to apply for U.S. citizenship, or U.S. nationality at all, without permanent residence in another U.S. jurisdiction.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> In addition, foreign nationals who do have <a href="/wiki/Green_card" title="Green card">lawful permanent residence in the United States</a> may be considered to have abandoned it if they have moved to live in American Samoa, and time spent there does not count in the required period of U.S. presence for naturalization.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>U.S. nationals without U.S. citizenship (the status of most American Samoans) have the right to reside in all parts of the United States without immigration restrictions. They also have the same rights as lawful permanent residents to sponsor foreign family members to immigrate to the United States (they may sponsor spouses and unmarried children), but not the same rights as U.S. citizens (who may also sponsor parents, married children, and siblings).<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Land_ownership">Land ownership</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Land ownership">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Under American Samoan law, land ownership is subject to racial restrictions.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Since 1900, there have been three main categories of land ownership: native, individual, and freehold. Native land, which makes up over 90% of all land in the territory, is land under the communal ownership of an <a href="/wiki/%CA%BBAiga" title="ʻAiga">ʻaiga</a>, as opposed to the private ownership of an individual. Freehold land, which makes up only about 2% of the total, is land which was granted to foreigners before the U.S. took possession of the territory in 1900 and whose owners have not chosen to revert to native or individual land status.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> <sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The American Samoa Code (Annotated) prohibits the transfer of ownership (whether by sale or otherwise) of any land other than freehold land to any person who has less than one-half native Samoan blood, which in this context includes both American and Western Samoa.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> In addition, it is prohibited to transfer ownership of any native (communal) land to any person who is not a full-blooded native Samoan: this includes any person who has any non-native blood whatsoever, even if they are more than one-half native Samoan.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> <sup id="cite_ref-Radiolab_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Radiolab-100">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>In <i>Craddick v. Territorial Registrar</i>, 1 Am. Samoa 2d. 10, 14 (1980), the Appellate Division of the High Court of American Samoa held that while these laws created a classification based on race, they did not violate the guarantees of equal protection and due process contained in the U.S. Constitution and the Revised American Samoan Constitution. Given the cruciality of land ownership and the communal ownership structure to American Samoan culture, and the American Samoan government's vital and demonstrated interest in preserving Samoan land and culture, the Court found that the laws in question pursued a proper purpose rather than a discriminatory one, and, being necessary to achieve that purpose, were sufficiently justified and thus constitutional.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Official_protest_to_naming_of_neighboring_Samoa">Official protest to naming of neighboring Samoa</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Official protest to naming of neighboring Samoa">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The U.S. Embassy in Samoa notes that: "In July 1997 the Constitution was amended to change the country's name from Western Samoa to Samoa. Samoa had been known simply as Samoa in the United Nations since joining the organization in 1976. The neighboring U.S. territory of American Samoa protested the move, feeling that the change diminished its own Samoan identity. American Samoans still use the terms Western Samoa and Western Samoans."<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"/><table class="infobox"><tbody><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-image"><div role="img" class="noresize" style="width: 700px; line-height: 1; text-align: center; background-color: #ffffff; position: relative;"><a href="/wiki/File:Counties_of_American_Samoa.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Counties_of_American_Samoa.png/700px-Counties_of_American_Samoa.png" decoding="async" width="700" height="284" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Counties_of_American_Samoa.png/1050px-Counties_of_American_Samoa.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Counties_of_American_Samoa.png/1400px-Counties_of_American_Samoa.png 2x" data-file-width="1981" data-file-height="803" /></a>

</div><div class="infobox-caption"><span style="position:relative; bottom:-3px"><i>Islands</i>, <i>atolls</i>, <b>districts</b> and counties of American Samoa<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;d&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_American_Samoa" title="Administrative divisions of American Samoa">Administrative divisions of American Samoa</a></div>
<p>American Samoa is administratively divided into three <a href="/wiki/District" title="District">districts</a>&#160;&#8211;&#32;<a href="/wiki/Western_District,_American_Samoa" title="Western District, American Samoa">Western</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eastern_District,_American_Samoa" title="Eastern District, American Samoa">Eastern</a> and <a href="/wiki/Manu%CA%BBa_District" title="Manuʻa District">Manuʻa</a>&#160;&#8211;&#32;and two "unorganized" atolls, <a href="/wiki/Swains_Island" title="Swains Island">Swains Island</a> and the uninhabited <a href="/wiki/Rose_Atoll" title="Rose Atoll">Rose Atoll</a>. The districts are subdivided into counties and villages. <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago" title="Pago Pago">Pago Pago</a>, often cited as the capital of American Samoa,<sup id="cite_ref-capital_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-capital-2">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup> is one of the largest villages and is located on the central part of Tutuila island in <a href="/wiki/Ma%CA%BBop%C5%ABtasi_County" title="Maʻopūtasi County">Maʻoputasi County</a>.
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:252px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Aq-map.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Aq-map.png/250px-Aq-map.png" decoding="async" width="250" height="268" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/07/Aq-map.png 1.5x" data-file-width="331" data-file-height="355" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Aq-map.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map of American Samoa</div></div></div>
<p>American Samoa, located within the geographical region of <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">Oceania</a>, is one of only two possessions of the United States in the <a href="/wiki/Southern_Hemisphere" title="Southern Hemisphere">Southern Hemisphere</a>, the other being <a href="/wiki/Jarvis_Island" title="Jarvis Island">Jarvis Island</a>. Its total land area is 76.1 square miles (197.1&#160;km<sup>2</sup>)—slightly larger than <a href="/wiki/Washington,_D.C." title="Washington, D.C.">Washington, D.C.</a>—consisting of five rugged, <a href="/wiki/High_island" title="High island">volcanic islands</a> and two coral <a href="/wiki/Atoll" title="Atoll">atolls</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DOI_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DOI-110">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pola_island.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/Pola_island.jpg/220px-Pola_island.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/Pola_island.jpg/330px-Pola_island.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6c/Pola_island.jpg/440px-Pola_island.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4032" data-file-height="3024" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pola_island.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Cockscomb Point on <a href="/wiki/Pola_Island" title="Pola Island">Pola Island</a> is seen jutting into the ocean.</div></div></div>
<p>The five volcanic islands are <a href="/wiki/Tutuila" title="Tutuila">Tutuila</a>, <a href="/wiki/Aunu%CA%BBu" title="Aunuʻu">Aunuʻu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ofu-Olosega" title="Ofu-Olosega">Ofu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ofu-Olosega" title="Ofu-Olosega">Olosega</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Ta%CA%BB%C5%AB" title="Taʻū">Taʻū</a>. The coral atolls are <a href="/wiki/Swains_Island" title="Swains Island">Swains</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rose_Atoll" title="Rose Atoll">Rose Atoll</a>. Of the seven islands, Rose Atoll is the only <a href="/wiki/Desert_island" title="Desert island">uninhabited</a> one; it is a <a href="/wiki/National_Monument_(United_States)" class="mw-redirect" title="National Monument (United States)">Marine National Monument</a>. American Samoa is the southernmost reach of the United States at fourteen degrees below the <a href="/wiki/Equator" title="Equator">equator</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ofu_Beach_American_Samoa_US_National_Park_Service.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Ofu_Beach_American_Samoa_US_National_Park_Service.jpg/220px-Ofu_Beach_American_Samoa_US_National_Park_Service.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Ofu_Beach_American_Samoa_US_National_Park_Service.jpg/330px-Ofu_Beach_American_Samoa_US_National_Park_Service.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Ofu_Beach_American_Samoa_US_National_Park_Service.jpg/440px-Ofu_Beach_American_Samoa_US_National_Park_Service.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4288" data-file-height="2848" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ofu_Beach_American_Samoa_US_National_Park_Service.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A view of American Samoa's Ofu Beach on <a href="/wiki/Ofu-Olosega" title="Ofu-Olosega">Ofu Island</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Manu%CA%BBa_Islands" title="Manuʻa Islands">Manuʻa Islands</a></div></div></div>
<p>Due to its positioning in the South <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ocean" title="Pacific Ocean">Pacific Ocean</a>, it is frequently hit by <a href="/wiki/Tropical_cyclones" class="mw-redirect" title="Tropical cyclones">tropical cyclones</a> between November and April. Rose Atoll is the easternmost point of the territory. American Samoa's <a href="/wiki/Rose_Atoll" title="Rose Atoll">Rose Atoll</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Extreme_points_of_the_United_States" class="mw-redirect" title="Extreme points of the United States">southernmost point of the United States</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> American Samoa is home to the <a href="/wiki/National_Park_of_American_Samoa" title="National Park of American Samoa">National Park of American Samoa</a>.
</p><p>The highest mountains are: <a href="/wiki/Lata_Mountain" title="Lata Mountain">Lata Mountain</a> (<a href="/wiki/Ta%CA%BB%C5%AB" title="Taʻū">Taʻū</a>), 3,170&#160;ft (970&#160;m); <a href="/wiki/Matafao_Peak" title="Matafao Peak">Matafao Peak</a>, 2,141&#160;ft (653&#160;m); Piumafua (<a href="/wiki/Olosega" class="mw-redirect" title="Olosega">Olosega</a>), 2,095&#160;ft (639&#160;m); and Tumutumu (<a href="/wiki/Ofu-Olosega" title="Ofu-Olosega">Ofu</a>), 1,621&#160;ft (494&#160;m). <a href="/wiki/Mount_Pioa" class="mw-redirect" title="Mount Pioa">Mount Pioa</a>, nicknamed the Rainmaker, is 1,718&#160;ft (524&#160;m).<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 3">&#58;&#8202;3&#8202;</span></sup> American Samoa is also home to some of the world's highest sea cliffs at 3,000&#160;ft (910&#160;m).<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Vatia_from_the_National_Park_of_American_Samoa.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Vatia_from_the_National_Park_of_American_Samoa.jpg/220px-Vatia_from_the_National_Park_of_American_Samoa.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Vatia_from_the_National_Park_of_American_Samoa.jpg/330px-Vatia_from_the_National_Park_of_American_Samoa.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Vatia_from_the_National_Park_of_American_Samoa.jpg/440px-Vatia_from_the_National_Park_of_American_Samoa.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3264" data-file-height="1836" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Vatia_from_the_National_Park_of_American_Samoa.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Coastline of American Samoa (in <a href="/wiki/Vatia,_American_Samoa" title="Vatia, American Samoa">Vatia</a>)</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Vailulu%CA%BBu" title="Vailuluʻu">Vailuluʻu</a> seamount, an <a href="/wiki/Submarine_volcano" title="Submarine volcano">active submerged volcano</a>, lies 28 miles (45&#160;km) east of <a href="/wiki/Ta%CA%BB%C5%AB" title="Taʻū">Taʻū</a> in American Samoa. It was discovered in 1975 and has since been studied by an international team of scientists, contributing towards understanding of the Earth's fundamental processes.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> Growing inside the <a href="/wiki/Volcanic_crater" title="Volcanic crater">summit crater</a> of Vailuluʻu is an active underwater <a href="/wiki/Volcanic_cone" title="Volcanic cone">volcanic cone</a>, named after Samoa's goddess of war, <a href="/wiki/Nafanua" title="Nafanua">Nafanua</a>.
</p><p>American Samoa lies within two terrestrial ecoregions: <a href="/wiki/Samoan_tropical_moist_forests" title="Samoan tropical moist forests">Samoan tropical moist forests</a> and <a href="/wiki/Western_Polynesian_tropical_moist_forests" title="Western Polynesian tropical moist forests">Western Polynesian tropical moist forests</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>American Samoa has a <a href="/wiki/Tropical_climate" title="Tropical climate">tropical climate</a> all year round with two distinct <a href="/wiki/Season#Tropics" title="Season">seasons</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Wet_season" title="Wet season">wet</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dry_season" title="Dry season">dry season</a>. The wet season is usually between December and March and the dry season is from April through to September with the average daily temperature around 81–83&#160;°F (27–28&#160;°C) all year round.
</p><p>The climate is warm, tropical, and humid, averaging around 80&#160;°F or 26.7&#160;°C, with a variation of about 15&#160;°F or 8&#160;°C during the year. The southern hemisphere winter, from June to September, is the coolest time of the year. The summer months of December to March bring hotter temperatures, while the months from April to November are considered the “dry” season. Throughout the year, however, rain follows clouds blown in by the trade winds that rise from the east almost daily. The mountains of the <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago" title="Pago Pago">Pago Pago</a> area, standing protectively over <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago_Harbor" title="Pago Pago Harbor">Pago Pago Harbor</a>, catch these clouds, bringing an average of 200 inches or 5,100 millimeters of rainfall per year.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 4">&#58;&#8202;4&#8202;</span></sup>
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate_change">Climate change</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Climate change">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="excerpt-block"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1066933788">.mw-parser-output .excerpt-hat .mw-editsection-like{font-style:normal}</style><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Climate_change_in_American_Samoa" title="Climate change in American Samoa">Climate change in American Samoa</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Climate_change_in_American_Samoa&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:NPS_american-samoa-context-map.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/NPS_american-samoa-context-map.jpg/220px-NPS_american-samoa-context-map.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="220" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/NPS_american-samoa-context-map.jpg/330px-NPS_american-samoa-context-map.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/10/NPS_american-samoa-context-map.jpg/440px-NPS_american-samoa-context-map.jpg 2x" data-file-width="471" data-file-height="471" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:NPS_american-samoa-context-map.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Locations of the Samoan Islands, including American Samoa</div></div></div>
<a href="/wiki/Climate_change_in_American_Samoa" title="Climate change in American Samoa">Climate change in American Samoa</a> encompasses the effects of <a href="/wiki/Climate_change" title="Climate change">climate change</a>, attributed to man-made increases in atmospheric <a href="/wiki/Carbon_dioxide" title="Carbon dioxide">carbon dioxide</a>, in the U.S. territory of American Samoa. The American Samoa Environmental Protection Agency (ASEPA) notes that the territory "has a fragile ecosystem" which is "directly and immediately impacted by global climate change".<sup id="cite_ref-Climate_change_in_American_Samoa_ASEPA_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Climate_change_in_American_Samoa_ASEPA-119">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup></div></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_American_Samoa" title="Economy of American Samoa">Economy of American Samoa</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:American_Samoa_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/American_Samoa_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/300px-American_Samoa_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="300" height="195" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/American_Samoa_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/450px-American_Samoa_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/American_Samoa_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/600px-American_Samoa_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:American_Samoa_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of American Samoa exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Line4392_-_Flickr_-_NOAA_Photo_Library.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Line4392_-_Flickr_-_NOAA_Photo_Library.jpg/220px-Line4392_-_Flickr_-_NOAA_Photo_Library.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Line4392_-_Flickr_-_NOAA_Photo_Library.jpg/330px-Line4392_-_Flickr_-_NOAA_Photo_Library.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Line4392_-_Flickr_-_NOAA_Photo_Library.jpg/440px-Line4392_-_Flickr_-_NOAA_Photo_Library.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Line4392_-_Flickr_-_NOAA_Photo_Library.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Tuna boats in the port of <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago" title="Pago Pago">Pago Pago</a></div></div></div>
<p>The economic health of American Samoa reflects the trends in other populated U.S. territories, which are in turn dependent on federal appropriations. Federal dollars enter the economy through congressional appropriations, categorical grants, <a href="/wiki/Social_Security_(United_States)" title="Social Security (United States)">Social Security</a> payments, and payments to Samoans retired from the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Military" class="mw-redirect" title="United States Military">military</a>. Tuna canning is the backbone of the American Samoa economy. Cannery employment and local auxiliary businesses provide additional revenues for the territorial government. In the mid-1960s, efforts began to develop a tourism industry in American Samoa. These efforts have been delayed due to issues such as inconsistent airline services, insufficient high-quality accommodations, and the lack of well-trained workers in the hospitality and tourism industries. Agriculture and fishing still provide sustenance for local families.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 8–9">&#58;&#8202;8–9&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>Employment on the island falls into three relatively equal-sized categories of approximately 5,000 workers each: the <a href="/wiki/Public_sector" title="Public sector">public sector</a>, the single remaining <a href="/wiki/Tuna" title="Tuna">tuna</a> <a href="/wiki/Cannery" class="mw-redirect" title="Cannery">cannery</a>, and the rest of the <a href="/wiki/Private_sector" title="Private sector">private sector</a>.
</p><p>There are only a few <a href="/wiki/Federal_government_of_the_United_States" title="Federal government of the United States">federal</a> employees in American Samoa and a few <a href="/wiki/Active_duty" title="Active duty">active duty</a> military personnel, except members of the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Coast_Guard" title="United States Coast Guard">U.S. Coast Guard</a>, military recruiters, and some Full-Time Support staff at the Pele Army Reserve unit that maintains the facility and provides cadre, training, and logistics support. The Pele US Army Reserve Center is in <a href="/wiki/Tafuna,_American_Samoa" title="Tafuna, American Samoa">Tafuna</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> and a <a href="/wiki/United_States_Army" title="United States Army">U.S. Army</a> and <a href="/wiki/United_States_Marine_Corps" title="United States Marine Corps">United States Marine Corps</a> recruiting station is in <a href="/wiki/Nu%27uuli,_American_Samoa" title="Nu&#39;uuli, American Samoa">Nuʻuuli</a>.
</p><p>There are six Army Reserve units at Pele:<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>Bravo Company, 100th Battalion, 442nd Infantry</li>
<li>Charlie Company, 100th Battalion, 442nd Infantry</li>
<li>411th Forward Support Company (Engineer)</li>
<li>USAR Theater Support Group Detachment American Samoa</li>
<li>1st Evacuation/Mortuary Platoon, 2nd Platoon, 962nd Quartermaster Company</li>
<li>127th Chaplain Detachment</li></ul>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Charlie_Tuna_AmSamoa.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Charlie_Tuna_AmSamoa.JPG/220px-Charlie_Tuna_AmSamoa.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="161" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Charlie_Tuna_AmSamoa.JPG/330px-Charlie_Tuna_AmSamoa.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Charlie_Tuna_AmSamoa.JPG/440px-Charlie_Tuna_AmSamoa.JPG 2x" data-file-width="954" data-file-height="699" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Charlie_Tuna_AmSamoa.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Mascot "<a href="/wiki/Charlie_the_Tuna" title="Charlie the Tuna">Charlie the Tuna</a>" at the <a href="/wiki/StarKist" title="StarKist">StarKist</a> cannery in <a href="/wiki/Atu%27u" title="Atu&#39;u">Atuʻu</a></div></div></div>
<p>The overwhelming majority of public sector employees work for the American Samoa territorial government. One tuna cannery is <a href="/wiki/StarKist_Tuna" class="mw-redirect" title="StarKist Tuna">StarKist</a>, which exports several hundred million dollars worth of canned tuna to the United States each year. In early 2007, the Samoan economy was highlighted in the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Congress" title="United States Congress">Congress</a> at the request of <a href="/wiki/Eni_Faleomavaega" title="Eni Faleomavaega">Eni Faleomavaega</a>, the Samoan delegate to the <a href="/wiki/United_States_House_of_Representatives" title="United States House of Representatives">United States House of Representatives</a>, as it was not mentioned in the <a href="/wiki/Minimum_wage" title="Minimum wage">minimum wage</a> bill. It was given no exemption from the coming increases, which he protested as unfair to the Samoan economy. House Speaker <a href="/wiki/Nancy_Pelosi" title="Nancy Pelosi">Nancy Pelosi</a> initially granted his request for an exemption, but backed down after being accused of serving special interests, since tuna packing company <a href="/wiki/Chicken_of_the_Sea" title="Chicken of the Sea">Chicken of the Sea</a> was based in her district. Samoa Packing, a <a href="/wiki/Chicken_of_the_Sea" title="Chicken of the Sea">Chicken of the Sea</a> <a href="/wiki/Subsidiary" title="Subsidiary">subsidiary</a> closed in 2009, citing both <a href="/wiki/Minimum_wage_in_the_United_States" title="Minimum wage in the United States">minimum wage</a> increases and increasing foreign competition, with the latter as the "main reason". Minimum wage in Samoa has been the topic of much debate, with the Samoan government and Chamber of Commerce strongly opposed, while businesses and workers hold nuanced views.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From 2002 to 2007, <a href="/wiki/Real_gross_domestic_product" title="Real gross domestic product">real GDP</a> of American Samoa increased at an average annual rate of 0.4 percent. The annual growth rates of real GDP ranged from −2.9 percent to +2.1 percent. The volatility in the growth rates of real GDP was primarily accounted for by changes in the exports of canned tuna. The tuna canning industry was the largest private employer in American Samoa during this period. In 2017, GDP in American Samoa decreased by 5.8%, but in 2018 it increased by 2.2%.<sup id="cite_ref-bea2_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bea2-124">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output <p>From 2002 to 2007, the population of American Samoa increased at an average annual rate of 2.3 percent, and real GDP per capita decreased at an average annual rate of 1.9 percent.
</p><p>Agricultural production serves as a cover for domestic needs and only a small share of fruits and vegetables are exported. According to figures as of 2013, the ratio between import and export is almost balanced. Many residents rely on transfer payments from relatives living on the mainland or from federal subsidies.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Deserted_Beach_near_Tisa%27s_Barefoot_Bar_-_panoramio.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Deserted_Beach_near_Tisa%27s_Barefoot_Bar_-_panoramio.jpg/220px-Deserted_Beach_near_Tisa%27s_Barefoot_Bar_-_panoramio.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Deserted_Beach_near_Tisa%27s_Barefoot_Bar_-_panoramio.jpg/330px-Deserted_Beach_near_Tisa%27s_Barefoot_Bar_-_panoramio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/55/Deserted_Beach_near_Tisa%27s_Barefoot_Bar_-_panoramio.jpg/440px-Deserted_Beach_near_Tisa%27s_Barefoot_Bar_-_panoramio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2816" data-file-height="2112" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Deserted_Beach_near_Tisa%27s_Barefoot_Bar_-_panoramio.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Tisa's Barefoot Bar &amp; Grill</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Fair_Labor_Standards_Act" class="mw-redirect" title="Fair Labor Standards Act">Fair Labor Standards Act of 1938</a> has contained special provisions for American Samoa since its inception, citing its limited economy.<sup id="cite_ref-flsa205_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-flsa205-126">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> American Samoan wages are based on the recommendations of a Special Industry Committee meeting bi-annually.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> Originally, the act contained provisions for other territories, provisions which were phased out as those territories developed more diverse economies.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2007, the <a href="/wiki/Fair_Minimum_Wage_Act_of_2007" title="Fair Minimum Wage Act of 2007">Fair Minimum Wage Act of 2007</a> was passed, increasing the minimum wage in American Samoa by 50¢ per hour in 2007 and another 50¢ per hour each year thereafter until the minimum wage in American Samoa equals the <a href="/wiki/Minimum_wage_in_the_United_States" title="Minimum wage in the United States">federal minimum wage</a> of $7.25 per hour in the United States.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> In response to the minimum wage increase, the <a href="/wiki/Chicken_of_the_Sea" title="Chicken of the Sea">Chicken of the Sea</a> tuna canning plant was shut down in 2009, and 2,041 employees were laid off in the process.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> The other major tuna canning plant in American Samoa is <a href="/wiki/StarKist" title="StarKist">StarKist</a>, which began laying off workers in August 2010, with plans to lay off a total of 800 workers due to the minimum wage increases and other rising operation costs.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> American Samoa Governor <a href="/wiki/Togiola_Tulafono" title="Togiola Tulafono">Togiola Tulafono</a> suggested that, rather than laying off minimum wage workers, the companies could reduce salaries and bonuses of top-tier employees.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Unemployment" title="Unemployment">unemployment rate</a> was 29.8% in 2005, but improved to 23.8% as of 2010<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>. In 2020, American Samoa's GDP was $709<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>million.<sup id="cite_ref-worldbank_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-worldbank-5">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Its GDP per capita (PPP) was $11,200 as of 2016<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some aspects of <a href="/wiki/Telecommunications_in_American_Samoa" title="Telecommunications in American Samoa">telecommunications in American Samoa</a> are, like other <a href="/wiki/United_States_territories" class="mw-redirect" title="United States territories">U.S. territories</a>, inferior to that of the mainland United States; a recent estimate showed that American Samoa's Internet speed is slower than that of several <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Europe" title="Eastern Europe">Eastern European</a> countries.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Taxation">Taxation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Taxation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>As in other U.S. territories, the U.S. federal government imposes <a href="/wiki/Federal_Insurance_Contributions_Act_tax" class="mw-redirect" title="Federal Insurance Contributions Act tax">payroll taxes</a><sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> and the equivalent self-employment tax<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> on income from work in American Samoa, but not the <a href="/wiki/Income_tax_in_the_United_States" title="Income tax in the United States">federal income tax</a> on income generated in American Samoa by its residents (except from work as U.S. government employees).<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Instead, the government of American Samoa itself taxes the worldwide income of its residents, as well as the income generated there by nonresidents, largely under the same rules and rates as the U.S. tax code in effect in 2000,<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> with certain modifications such as a minimum tax rate of 4%.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> A similar situation applies to corporations.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> In 1983, the use of citizenship in taxation by American Samoa (due to its incorporation of the U.S. tax code) was ruled unconstitutional.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The U.S. federal government does not impose <a href="/wiki/Estate_tax_in_the_United_States" title="Estate tax in the United States">estate</a> or <a href="/wiki/Gift_tax_in_the_United_States" title="Gift tax in the United States">gift taxes</a> on property not located in the United States (states and <a href="/wiki/District_of_Columbia" class="mw-redirect" title="District of Columbia">District of Columbia</a>) owned by residents of a U.S. territory (including American Samoa) who are not U.S. citizens or who acquired U.S. citizenship by birth or naturalization in that same U.S. territory.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> However, these taxes still apply to residents of a U.S. territory who acquired U.S. citizenship by birth or naturalization in a different part of the U.S. or by descent.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> It has been argued that this distinction based on place of birth, and not only residence or citizenship, is a rare case of unconstitutional tax discrimination, but it has never been challenged in court.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> The government of American Samoa itself does not impose estate or gift taxes.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Unlike U.S. citizens, U.S. nationals without U.S. citizenship (the status of most American Samoans) who do not reside in the United States or any U.S. territory enjoy the unique combination of maintaining a <a href="/wiki/United_States_passport" title="United States passport">U.S. passport</a> and the right of return to the U.S. while not being subject to U.S. federal income tax on their non-U.S. income,<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> or to U.S. federal estate or gift taxes on their non-U.S. property.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> U.S. citizens (or anyone) cannot acquire this status after birth.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>American Samoa does not impose a sales tax, but it imposes a general import tax of 8%.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> American Samoa is an independent <a href="/wiki/Customs_territory" title="Customs territory">customs territory</a>, whose importation rules and taxes differ from those applicable to other parts of the United States.<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Telecommunications">Telecommunications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Telecommunications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2012 Michael Calabrese, Daniel Calarco, and Colin Richardson stated that American Samoa had the most expensive internet of any U.S. territory and that the speeds were only slightly superior to those of <a href="/wiki/Dial-up_internet" class="mw-redirect" title="Dial-up internet">dial-up internet</a> in the U.S. Mainland in the 1990s. They also stated that many American Samoans are too poor to afford "high-speed internet".<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transportation">Transportation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Transportation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:American_Samoa_license_plate_2011_5504.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/American_Samoa_license_plate_2011_5504.png/220px-American_Samoa_license_plate_2011_5504.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="111" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/52/American_Samoa_license_plate_2011_5504.png/330px-American_Samoa_license_plate_2011_5504.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/52/American_Samoa_license_plate_2011_5504.png 2x" data-file-width="392" data-file-height="197" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:American_Samoa_license_plate_2011_5504.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The current <a href="/wiki/Vehicle_registration_plates_of_American_Samoa" title="Vehicle registration plates of American Samoa">territorial license plate</a> design, introduced in 2011</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:American_Samoa_Highway_001.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/American_Samoa_Highway_001.svg/170px-American_Samoa_Highway_001.svg.png" decoding="async" width="170" height="170" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/American_Samoa_Highway_001.svg/255px-American_Samoa_Highway_001.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/American_Samoa_Highway_001.svg/340px-American_Samoa_Highway_001.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="300" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:American_Samoa_Highway_001.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>American Samoa Route Marker – Main Road</div></div></div>
<p>American Samoa has 150 miles (240<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>km) of <a href="/wiki/List_of_highways_in_American_Samoa" title="List of highways in American Samoa">highways</a> (estimated in 2008).<sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> The maximum speed limit is 30 miles per hour.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Port" title="Port">Ports</a> and <a href="/wiki/Harbor" title="Harbor">harbors</a> include <a href="/wiki/Aunu%CA%BBu" title="Aunuʻu">Aunuʻu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Auasi" class="mw-redirect" title="Auasi">Auasi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Faleasao" title="Faleasao">Faleasao</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ofu-Olosega" title="Ofu-Olosega">Ofu</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago" title="Pago Pago">Pago Pago</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> American Samoa has no railways.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> The territory has three <a href="/wiki/Airport" title="Airport">airports</a>, all of which have paved <a href="/wiki/Runway" title="Runway">runways</a>. The main airport is <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago_International_Airport" title="Pago Pago International Airport">Pago Pago International Airport</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> on the island of Tutuila. The Manuʻa group has two airports: <a href="/wiki/Ofu_Airport" title="Ofu Airport">Ofu Airport</a> on the island of Ofu, and <a href="/wiki/Fitiuta_Airport" title="Fitiuta Airport">Fitiuta Airport</a> on the island of Taʻū. According to a 1999 estimate, the territory has no <a href="/wiki/Merchant_marine" class="mw-redirect" title="Merchant marine">merchant marine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On June 8, 1922, the first bus service on Tutuila began its operations.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> There is currently a bus system in American Samoa called the <i>ʻaiga</i> bus system—it consists of buses that travel across the island of <a href="/wiki/Tutuila" title="Tutuila">Tutuila</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div style="clear:left;"></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_American_Samoa" title="Demographics of American Samoa">Demographics of American Samoa</a></div>
<p>As of 2022, the population of American Samoa is estimated around 45,443 people.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/2020_United_States_census" title="2020 United States census">2020 census</a> counted 49,710 people, 97.5% of whom lived on the largest island, <a href="/wiki/Tutuila" title="Tutuila">Tutuila</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-census-counties_4-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-counties-4">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup> About 57.6% of the population were born in American Samoa, 28.6% in independent <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a>, 6.1% in other parts of the United States, 4.5% in <a href="/wiki/Asia" title="Asia">Asia</a>, 2.9% in other parts of <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">Oceania</a>, and 0.2% elsewhere. At least 69% of the population had a parent born outside American Samoa.<sup id="cite_ref-census-social_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-social-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>American Samoa is small enough to have just one <a href="/wiki/ZIP_code" class="mw-redirect" title="ZIP code">ZIP code</a>, 96799, and uses the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Postal_Service" title="United States Postal Service">U.S. Postal Service</a> (state code "AS") for mail delivery.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnicity_and_language">Ethnicity and language</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Ethnicity and language">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the 2020 census, 89.4% of the population reported at least partial <a href="/wiki/Samoans" title="Samoans">Samoan</a> ethnicity, 83.2% only Samoan, 5.8% Asian, 5.5% other <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Islander" title="Pacific Islander">Pacific island ethnicities</a>, 4.4% <a href="/wiki/Miscegenation" title="Miscegenation">mixed</a>, and 1.1% other ethnicities.<sup id="cite_ref-census-general_164-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-general-164">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Samoan_language" title="Samoan language">Samoan language</a> was spoken at home by 87.9% of the population, while 6.1% spoke other <a href="/wiki/Oceanic_languages" title="Oceanic languages">Pacific island languages</a>, 3.3% spoke <a href="/wiki/English_language" title="English language">English</a>, 2.1% spoke an <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Asia" title="Languages of Asia">Asian language</a>, and 0.5% spoke other languages; 47.2% of the population spoke English at home or "very well".<sup id="cite_ref-census-social_7-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-social-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> In 2022, Samoan and English were designated as official languages of the territory.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> At least some of the <a href="/wiki/Hearing_loss" title="Hearing loss">deaf</a> population use <a href="/wiki/Samoan_Sign_Language" title="Samoan Sign Language">Samoan Sign Language</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:ZION_CHURCH_IN_LEONE,_AMERICAN_SAMOA.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/ZION_CHURCH_IN_LEONE%2C_AMERICAN_SAMOA.jpg/220px-ZION_CHURCH_IN_LEONE%2C_AMERICAN_SAMOA.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="148" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/ZION_CHURCH_IN_LEONE%2C_AMERICAN_SAMOA.jpg/330px-ZION_CHURCH_IN_LEONE%2C_AMERICAN_SAMOA.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/ZION_CHURCH_IN_LEONE%2C_AMERICAN_SAMOA.jpg/440px-ZION_CHURCH_IN_LEONE%2C_AMERICAN_SAMOA.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5260" data-file-height="3528" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:ZION_CHURCH_IN_LEONE,_AMERICAN_SAMOA.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Zion Church in <a href="/wiki/Leone,_American_Samoa" title="Leone, American Samoa">Leone</a></div></div></div>
<p>Major <a href="/wiki/Christian_denomination" title="Christian denomination">Christian denominations</a> on the island include the <a href="/wiki/Congregational_Christian_Church_in_American_Samoa" title="Congregational Christian Church in American Samoa">Congregational Christian Church in American Samoa</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a>, The <a href="/wiki/Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints" class="mw-redirect" title="Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints">Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints</a>, and the Methodist Church of Samoa. Collectively, these churches account for the vast majority of the population.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/J._Gordon_Melton" title="J. Gordon Melton">J. Gordon Melton</a> in his book claims that the <a href="/wiki/Methodists" class="mw-redirect" title="Methodists">Methodists</a>, <a href="/wiki/Congregationalists" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregationalists">Congregationalists</a> with the <a href="/wiki/London_Missionary_Society" title="London Missionary Society">London Missionary Society</a>, and Roman Catholics led the first Christian missions to the islands. Other denominations arrived later, beginning in 1895 with the <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventists" class="mw-redirect" title="Seventh-day Adventists">Seventh-day Adventists</a>, various <a href="/wiki/Pentecostals" class="mw-redirect" title="Pentecostals">Pentecostals</a> (including the <a href="/wiki/Assemblies_of_God" title="Assemblies of God">Assemblies of God</a>), <a href="/wiki/Church_of_the_Nazarene" title="Church of the Nazarene">Church of the Nazarene</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witnesses">Jehovah's Witnesses</a> and The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Samoan_church_4.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Samoan_church_4.JPG/220px-Samoan_church_4.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Samoan_church_4.JPG/330px-Samoan_church_4.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Samoan_church_4.JPG/440px-Samoan_church_4.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1578" data-file-height="1170" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Samoan_church_4.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>One of many churches in Samoa</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/CIA_Factbook" class="mw-redirect" title="CIA Factbook">CIA Factbook</a> 2010 estimate shows the religious affiliations of American Samoa as 98.3% <a href="/wiki/Christians" title="Christians">Christian</a>, other 1%, unaffiliated 0.7%.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> World Christian Database 2010 estimate shows the religious affiliations of American Samoa as 98.3% Christian, 0.7% <a href="/wiki/Agnosticism" title="Agnosticism">agnostic</a>, 0.4% <a href="/wiki/Chinese_folk_religion" title="Chinese folk religion">Chinese Universalist</a>, 0.3% <a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhist</a> and 0.3% followers of the <a href="/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith" title="Baháʼí Faith">Baháʼí Faith</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to <a href="/wiki/Pew_Research_Center" title="Pew Research Center">Pew Research Center</a>, 98.3% of the total population is Christian. Among Christians, 59.5% are <a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a>, 19.7% are <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic">Roman Catholic</a> and 19.2% are <a href="/wiki/List_of_Christian_denominations" title="List of Christian denominations">other Christians</a>. A major Protestant church on the island, gathering a substantial part of the local Protestant population, is the <a href="/wiki/Congregational_Christian_Church_in_American_Samoa" title="Congregational Christian Church in American Samoa">Congregational Christian Church in American Samoa</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Reformed_church" class="mw-redirect" title="Reformed church">Reformed</a> denomination in the <a href="/wiki/Congregational_church" title="Congregational church">Congregationalist tradition</a>. As of August&#160;2017<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, <a href="/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints" title="The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints">The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints</a> website claims a membership of 16,180 (one-quarter of American Samoa's entire population), with 41 congregations and four family history centers.<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witnesses">Jehovah's Witnesses</a> claim 210 "ministers of the word" and three congregations.<sup id="cite_ref-168" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-168">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:American_samoa_community_college.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/American_samoa_community_college.jpg/220px-American_samoa_community_college.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="216" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/American_samoa_community_college.jpg/330px-American_samoa_community_college.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/American_samoa_community_college.jpg/440px-American_samoa_community_college.jpg 2x" data-file-width="450" data-file-height="442" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:American_samoa_community_college.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_Community_College" title="American Samoa Community College">American Samoa Community College</a></div></div></div>
<p>The island contains 23 <a href="/wiki/Primary_school" title="Primary school">primary schools</a>. Of the ten <a href="/wiki/Secondary_school" title="Secondary school">secondary schools</a>, five are operated by the <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_Department_of_Education" title="American Samoa Department of Education">American Samoa Department of Education</a>;<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> the other five are either administered by <a href="/wiki/Parochial_school" title="Parochial school">religious denominations</a> or are <a href="/wiki/Private_school" title="Private school">privately owned</a>. <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_Community_College" title="American Samoa Community College">American Samoa Community College</a>, founded in 1970, provides <a href="/wiki/Higher_education" title="Higher education">post-secondary education</a> on the islands.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Leone_high_school.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Leone_high_school.jpg/220px-Leone_high_school.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="138" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Leone_high_school.jpg/330px-Leone_high_school.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Leone_high_school.jpg/440px-Leone_high_school.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4032" data-file-height="2520" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Leone_high_school.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Leone_High_School" title="Leone High School">Leone High School</a></div></div></div>
<p>American Samoa was home to one high school as of 1961, which existed due to the matai's pressure on the naval governor to transform the old Marine barracks at <a href="/wiki/Utulei" class="mw-redirect" title="Utulei">Utulei</a> into a school. The teenagers of well-off and more politically connected families attended the school, which would later be known as <a href="/wiki/Samoana_High_School" title="Samoana High School">Samoana High School</a>. With a median age of 15, the demand for more high schools was increasing, and three new high schools were established by 1968. Another two soon followed, and by 1979, 2,800 high school students were attending six public and private high schools in American Samoa. Looking for a cost-effective way for educational reformation, Governor <a href="/wiki/H._Rex_Lee" title="H. Rex Lee">H. Rex Lee</a> introduced the public television system in 1964.<sup id="cite_ref-Ruck_41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Ruck-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Samoa" title="Culture of Samoa">Culture of Samoa</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Jean_p_haydon_museum.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Jean_p_haydon_museum.png/220px-Jean_p_haydon_museum.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Jean_p_haydon_museum.png/330px-Jean_p_haydon_museum.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Jean_p_haydon_museum.png/440px-Jean_p_haydon_museum.png 2x" data-file-width="1724" data-file-height="1140" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Jean_p_haydon_museum.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Jean_P._Haydon_Museum" title="Jean P. Haydon Museum">Jean P. Haydon Museum</a> in <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago" title="Pago Pago">Pago Pago</a></div></div></div>
<p>The Samoan culture has developed over 3,500 years and largely withstood interaction with European cultures. It was adapted well to the teachings of <a href="/wiki/Christianity_in_American_Samoa" class="mw-redirect" title="Christianity in American Samoa">Christianity</a>. The Samoan language is still in use in daily exchange; however, English is widely used and also the legal official language. Besides <a href="/wiki/Samoan_language" title="Samoan language">Samoan language</a> classes and cultural courses, all instructions in public schools are in English. The basic unit of the American Samoa culture is the <i>ʻaiga</i> (family). It consists of both immediate and extended family.
</p><p>The <i>matai</i>, or chief, is the head of the ʻaiga. The chief is the custodian of all ʻaiga properties. A village (nuʻu) is made up of several or many ʻaiga with a common or shared interest. Each ʻaiga is represented by their chief in the village councils.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 5–6">&#58;&#8202;5–6&#8202;</span></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="excerpt-block"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066933788"/><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable dablink excerpt-hat selfref">This section is an excerpt from <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Samoa" title="Music of Samoa">Music of Samoa</a>.<span class="mw-editsection-like plainlinks"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Music_of_Samoa&amp;action=edit">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><div class="excerpt">
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Samoan_lali_log_drums_at_Piula_Theological_College.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Samoan_lali_log_drums_at_Piula_Theological_College.JPG/220px-Samoan_lali_log_drums_at_Piula_Theological_College.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Samoan_lali_log_drums_at_Piula_Theological_College.JPG/330px-Samoan_lali_log_drums_at_Piula_Theological_College.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/25/Samoan_lali_log_drums_at_Piula_Theological_College.JPG/440px-Samoan_lali_log_drums_at_Piula_Theological_College.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Samoan_lali_log_drums_at_Piula_Theological_College.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Samoan <a href="/w/index.php?title=Talipalau&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Talipalau (page does not exist)">Talipalau</a> log drums at Piula Theological College, distant ancestor of the Fijian <a href="/wiki/Lali_(drum)" title="Lali (drum)">Lali</a> drums</div></div></div>
<p>Music of <a href="/wiki/Samoa" title="Samoa">Samoa</a> is a complex mix of cultures and traditions, with pre- and post-European contact histories. Since American colonization, popular traditions such as <a href="/wiki/Rap_music" class="mw-redirect" title="Rap music">rap</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hip_hop" class="mw-redirect" title="Hip hop">hip hop</a> have been integrated into Samoan music.
</p>
Traditional Samoan <a href="/wiki/Musical_instrument" title="Musical instrument">musical instruments</a> includes several different distinctive instruments, including a <a href="/wiki/Fala_(musical_instrument)" class="mw-redirect" title="Fala (musical instrument)">fala</a>, which is a rolled-up mat beaten with sticks and several types of <a href="/wiki/Slit_drum" title="Slit drum">slit drum</a>.</div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Sports_in_American_Samoa" title="Sports in American Samoa">Sports in American Samoa</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:South_pacific_games.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/South_pacific_games.jpg/220px-South_pacific_games.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="366" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/South_pacific_games.jpg/330px-South_pacific_games.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/97/South_pacific_games.jpg/440px-South_pacific_games.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1529" data-file-height="2545" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:South_pacific_games.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>American Samoa at the <a href="/wiki/South_Pacific_Games" class="mw-redirect" title="South Pacific Games">South Pacific Games</a></div></div></div>
<p>The main sports played in American Samoa are <a href="/wiki/American_football" title="American football">football</a>, <a href="/wiki/Samoan_cricket" class="mw-redirect" title="Samoan cricket">Samoan cricket</a>, <a href="/wiki/Canoeing" title="Canoeing">canoeing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Yachting" title="Yachting">yachting</a>, <a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">basketball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Golf" title="Golf">golf</a>, <a href="/wiki/Netball" title="Netball">netball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tennis" title="Tennis">tennis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rugby_football" title="Rugby football">rugby</a>, <a href="/wiki/Table_tennis" title="Table tennis">table tennis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Boxing" title="Boxing">boxing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bowling" title="Bowling">bowling</a>, <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a>, and fishing tournaments. Some current and former sports clubs are the American Samoa Tennis Association, Rugby Unions, Lavalava Golf Club, and Gamefish Association. Leagues improved and organized better after the completion of the <a href="/wiki/Veterans_Memorial_Stadium_(Pago_Pago)" title="Veterans Memorial Stadium (Pago Pago)">Veterans Memorial Stadium</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 338">&#58;&#8202;338&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/1997_South_Pacific_Mini_Games" title="1997 South Pacific Mini Games">1997 South Pacific Mini Games</a> were the biggest international event ever to take place in American Samoa. The bid to host the games for the 23 participating countries was approved in May 1993. In January 1994, Governor <a href="/wiki/A._P._Lutali" title="A. P. Lutali">A. P. Lutali</a> appointed Fuga Teleso to head the task force charged with game preparations, including the construction of a stadium. Groundbreaking was in January 1994. The Governor later handed the task force on preparations to Lieutenant Governor Togiola. The task force merged with the American Samoa National Olympics Committee to better coordinate and facilitate preparations. V.P. Willis Construction built the 1,500-seat stands. The <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_Department_of_Public_Safety" title="American Samoa Department of Public Safety">Department of Public Safety</a> trained its force for special games security. The opening ceremony became extravagant where the <a href="/wiki/U.S._Army_Reserve" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. Army Reserve">U.S. Army Reserve</a> carried the torch from <a href="/wiki/Tula,_American_Samoa" title="Tula, American Samoa">Tula</a> and <a href="/wiki/Leone,_American_Samoa" title="Leone, American Samoa">Leone</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 357–358">&#58;&#8202;357–358&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>About 2,000 athletes, coaches, and sponsors attended from 19 countries and competed in 11 sports at the game. American Samoa fielded a team of 248 athletes. The team won 48 medals, 22 of which were gold medals, and American Samoa came in fourth overall in the ratings. <a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa_Rotary_Club&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="American Samoa Rotary Club (page does not exist)">American Samoa Rotary Club</a> honored Fuga Tolani Teleso with the community's top award, the Paul Harris Fellowship Award, for his work on constructing the <a href="/wiki/Veterans_Memorial_Stadium_(Pago_Pago)" title="Veterans Memorial Stadium (Pago Pago)">Veterans Memorial Stadium</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 359">&#58;&#8202;359&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>In 1982, yachters competed in the Hobie World Championship held in <a href="/wiki/Tahiti" title="Tahiti">Tahiti</a>. American Samoa beat the <a href="/wiki/Apia" title="Apia">Apia</a> team by half a point and won the Samoa Cup. In 1983, a team coached by Dr. Adele Satele-Galeai brought home the winning trophy from the Regional women's volleyball tournament in <a href="/wiki/Hawaii" title="Hawaii">Hawaii</a>. Also in 1983, the <a href="/wiki/South_Pacific_Games" class="mw-redirect" title="South Pacific Games">South Pacific Games</a> were held in Apia. American Samoa received 13 medals: four gold, four silver, and five bronze. That same year, three junior golfers made the cut out of 1,000 players to attend the World Junior Golf Tournament in <a href="/wiki/San_Diego,_California" class="mw-redirect" title="San Diego, California">San Diego, California</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 338">&#58;&#8202;338&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>In 1987, American Samoa became the 167th member of the <a href="/wiki/International_Olympic_Committee" title="International Olympic Committee">International Olympic Committee</a>. The first South Pacific Junior Tennis Tournament was held at the <a href="/wiki/Tafuna,_American_Samoa" title="Tafuna, American Samoa">Tafuna</a> courts in January 1990.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 339">&#58;&#8202;339&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Tony_Solaita" title="Tony Solaita">Tony Solaita</a> was the first American Samoan to play in <a href="/wiki/Major_League_Baseball" title="Major League Baseball">Major League Baseball</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 339">&#58;&#8202;339&#8202;</span></sup> There are thirty players from American Samoa in the National Football League (NFL) as of 2015 and over 200 play Div.
