I <a href="/wiki/NCAA_Football" class="mw-redirect" title="NCAA Football">NCAA Football</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> Some American Samoan NFL football players are <a href="/wiki/Shalom_Luani" title="Shalom Luani">Shalom Luani</a>, <a href="/wiki/Junior_Siavii" title="Junior Siavii">Junior Siavii</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jonathan_Fanene" title="Jonathan Fanene">Jonathan Fanene</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mosi_Tatupu" title="Mosi Tatupu">Mosi Tatupu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shaun_Nua" title="Shaun Nua">Shaun Nua</a>, <a href="/wiki/Isaac_Sopoaga" title="Isaac Sopoaga">Isaac Sopoaga</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Daniel_Te%27o-Nesheim" title="Daniel Te&#39;o-Nesheim">Daniel Te'o-Nesheim</a>.
</p><p>After <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, a Welfare and Recreation Department was created. This department arranged bowling, softball, badminton tournaments, basketball, and volleyball at various Tutuila locations. Boxing matches and dancing also became popular activities.<sup id="cite_ref-171" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-171">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="American_football">American football</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: American football">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/American_football_in_American_Samoa" title="American football in American Samoa">American football in American Samoa</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:High_school_football_tutuila_samoa.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/High_school_football_tutuila_samoa.png/220px-High_school_football_tutuila_samoa.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="212" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/High_school_football_tutuila_samoa.png/330px-High_school_football_tutuila_samoa.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/High_school_football_tutuila_samoa.png/440px-High_school_football_tutuila_samoa.png 2x" data-file-width="1168" data-file-height="1126" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:High_school_football_tutuila_samoa.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>High school football game</div></div></div>
<p>About 30 ethnic Samoans, all from American Samoa, currently play in the <a href="/wiki/National_Football_League" title="National Football League">National Football League</a>, and more than 200 play <a href="/wiki/National_Collegiate_Athletic_Association" title="National Collegiate Athletic Association">NCAA</a> <a href="/wiki/Division_I_(NCAA)" class="mw-redirect" title="Division I (NCAA)">Division I</a> <a href="/wiki/College_football" title="College football">college football</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Football_Island_172-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Football_Island-172">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> In recent years, it has been estimated that a Samoan male (either an American Samoan, or a Samoan living in the mainland United States) is anywhere from 40<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> to 56 times<sup id="cite_ref-Football_Island_172-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Football_Island-172">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> more likely to play in the NFL than a non-Samoan American, giving American Samoa the nickname "Football Islands".<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> Samoans are the most disproportionately overrepresented ethnic group in the National Football League.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Six-time <a href="/wiki/All-Pro" title="All-Pro">All-Pro</a> <a href="/wiki/Junior_Seau" title="Junior Seau">Junior Seau</a> was one of the most famous Americans of Samoan heritage ever to play in the NFL, having been elected to the <a href="/wiki/NFL_1990s_All-Decade_Team" class="mw-redirect" title="NFL 1990s All-Decade Team">NFL 1990s All-Decade Team</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pro_Football_Hall_of_Fame" title="Pro Football Hall of Fame">Pro Football Hall of Fame</a>. <a href="/wiki/Pittsburgh_Steelers" title="Pittsburgh Steelers">Pittsburgh Steelers</a> safety <a href="/wiki/Troy_Polamalu" title="Troy Polamalu">Troy Polamalu</a>, though born and raised in the mainland United States, is another famous American of Samoan heritage to have played in the NFL, not having his hair cut since 2000 (and only because a <a href="/wiki/USC_Trojans_football" title="USC Trojans football">USC</a> coach told him he had to) and wearing it down during games in honor of his heritage. The football culture was featured on <i><a href="/wiki/60_Minutes" title="60 Minutes">60 Minutes</a></i> on January 17, 2010.
</p><p>At the <a href="/wiki/2016_Republican_National_Convention" title="2016 Republican National Convention">2016 Republican National Convention</a>, American Samoa's delegation said American Samoa is "the greatest exporter of NFL players".<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Association_football">Association football</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Association football">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_national_football_team" title="American Samoa national football team">American Samoa national football team</a> is one of the newest teams in the world and is also noted for being the world's weakest. They lost to <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a> <a href="/wiki/Australia_31%E2%80%930_American_Samoa" title="Australia 31–0 American Samoa">31–0</a> in a <a href="/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup" title="FIFA World Cup">FIFA World Cup</a> qualifying match on April 11, 2001, but on November 22, 2011, they finally won their first ever game, beating <a href="/wiki/Tonga" title="Tonga">Tonga</a> 2–1 in a <a href="/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup" title="FIFA World Cup">FIFA World Cup</a> qualifier.<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> The appearance of American Samoa's <a href="/wiki/Jaiyah_Saelua" title="Jaiyah Saelua">Jaiyah Saelua</a> in the contest "apparently became the first transgender player to compete on a World Cup stage".<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The American Samoan national team features in the highly rated 2014 <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">British</a> film <a href="/wiki/Next_Goal_Wins_(2014_film)" title="Next Goal Wins (2014 film)"><i>Next Goal Wins</i></a>. The film documents the team's <a href="/wiki/2014_FIFA_World_Cup_qualification_%E2%80%93_OFC_First_Round" title="2014 FIFA World Cup qualification – OFC First Round">2014 FIFA World Cup qualifying</a> campaign, in which they achieved their first-ever international win. Saelua and <a href="/wiki/Nicky_Salapu" title="Nicky Salapu">Nicky Salapu</a>, the man famous for being the <a href="/wiki/Goalkeeper_(association_football)" title="Goalkeeper (association football)">goalkeeper</a> during the team's 31–0 loss to Australia in 2001, feature prominently in the film.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> A <a href="/wiki/Next_Goal_Wins_(2023_film)" title="Next Goal Wins (2023 film)">feature film adaptation</a> of the documentary was planned, to be directed by <a href="/wiki/Taika_Waititi" title="Taika Waititi">Taika Waititi</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Rugby_league">Rugby league</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Rugby league">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_national_rugby_league_team" title="American Samoa national rugby league team">American Samoa national rugby league team</a> represents the country in international <a href="/wiki/Rugby_league" title="Rugby league">rugby league</a>. The team competed in the 1988, 1992, 1998 and 2004 <a href="/wiki/Rugby_League_Pacific_Cup" class="mw-redirect" title="Rugby League Pacific Cup">Pacific Cup</a> competitions. The team has also competed in the 2003 and 2004 <a href="/wiki/Rugby_League_World_Sevens" title="Rugby League World Sevens">World Sevens</a> qualifiers in the 2005 World Sevens. America Samoa's first match in the international Rugby League was in the 1988 <a href="/wiki/Rugby_League_Pacific_Cup" class="mw-redirect" title="Rugby League Pacific Cup">Pacific Cup</a> against <a href="/wiki/Tonga_national_rugby_league_team" title="Tonga national rugby league team">Tonga</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tonga_national_rugby_league_team" title="Tonga national rugby league team">Tonga</a> won the match 38–14 which is still the biggest loss by an American Samoan side. American Samoa's biggest win was in 2004 against <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia_national_rugby_league_team" title="New Caledonia national rugby league team">New Caledonia</a> with a final score of 62–6.
</p><p>American Samoa gets broadcasts of the <a href="/wiki/National_Rugby_League" title="National Rugby League">National Rugby League</a> in Australia on <a href="/wiki/Free-to-air" title="Free-to-air">free-to-air television</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-rugbyleagueplanet1_184-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rugbyleagueplanet1-184">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There is also a new movement that aims to set up a four-team domestic competition in American Samoa.<sup id="cite_ref-rugbyleagueplanet1_184-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rugbyleagueplanet1-184">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Rugby_union">Rugby union</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Rugby union">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p><a href="/wiki/Rugby_union_in_American_Samoa" title="Rugby union in American Samoa">Rugby union</a> is a growing sport in American Samoa. The first rugby game recorded in American Samoa was in 1924, since then the development of the game had been heavily overshadowed by the influence of <a href="/wiki/American_Football" class="mw-redirect" title="American Football">American Football</a> during the 1970s. The highest governing body of rugby in American Samoa is the <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_Rugby_Union" title="American Samoa Rugby Union">American Samoa Rugby Union</a> which was founded in 1990 and was not affiliated with the <a href="/wiki/World_Rugby" title="World Rugby">IRB</a> until 2012. Internationally, two American Samoans have played for the <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a> national rugby union team, known as the <a href="/wiki/All_Blacks" class="mw-redirect" title="All Blacks">All Blacks</a>. <a href="/wiki/Frank_Solomon" title="Frank Solomon">Frank Solomon</a> (born in <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago" title="Pago Pago">Pago Pago</a>) became the first American national of Samoan descent to play for a New Zealand team. Considered a pacific pioneer in New Zealand rugby,<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> Solomon scored a try against <a href="/wiki/Australia_national_rugby_union_team" title="Australia national rugby union team">Australia</a> in the inaugural <a href="/wiki/Bledisloe_Cup" title="Bledisloe Cup">Bledisloe Cup</a> match in 1932, which New Zealand won 21–13.
</p><p>The second American Samoan to play for the All Blacks is <a href="/wiki/Jerome_Kaino" title="Jerome Kaino">Jerome Kaino</a> (born in <a href="/wiki/Faga%27alu" class="mw-redirect" title="Faga&#39;alu">Fagaʻalu</a>). A native of <a href="/wiki/Leone,_American_Samoa" title="Leone, American Samoa">Leone</a>, Kaino moved to New Zealand when he was four. In 2004, at age 21, he played his first match for New Zealand against the <a href="/wiki/Barbarian_F.C." title="Barbarian F.C.">Barbarians</a> where he scored his first try, contributing to New Zealand's 47–19 victory that resulted in him becoming a man of the match.<sup id="cite_ref-nzhKaino_186-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nzhKaino-186">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup> He also played a crucial role in the <a href="/wiki/Rugby_World_Cup_2011" class="mw-redirect" title="Rugby World Cup 2011">Rugby World Cup 2011</a> playing every match in the tournament. He scored four tries in the event which led to New Zealand winning the final against <a href="/wiki/France_national_rugby_union_team" title="France national rugby union team">France</a> 8–7. Kaino was also a key member of the <a href="/wiki/Rugby_World_Cup_2015" class="mw-redirect" title="Rugby World Cup 2015">2015 Rugby World Cup</a> squad, where he played every match including a try he scored in the quarterfinals against <a href="/wiki/France_national_rugby_union_team" title="France national rugby union team">France</a> which New Zealand won 62–13. He scored again in the semifinals against <a href="/wiki/South_Africa_national_rugby_union_team" title="South Africa national rugby union team">South Africa</a>, which New Zealand won 20–18. He played in the World Cup final against <a href="/wiki/Australia_national_rugby_union_team" title="Australia national rugby union team">Australia</a> where New Zealand won again 34–17 to become world champions for a record three times (1987, 2011, and 2015). Kaino is one of twenty New Zealand rugby players to have won the <a href="/wiki/Rugby_World_Cup" title="Rugby World Cup">Rugby World Cup</a> twice, back to back in 2011 and 2015. In August 2015, the American Samoa Rugby Union Board selected Leota Toma Patu from the village of Leone as the coach for the Talavalu 15 men's team that represented American Samoa at the Ocean Cup 2015 in Papua New Guinea.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_sports">Other sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: Other sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<ul><li><b>Boxing</b>: <a href="/wiki/Maselino_Masoe" title="Maselino Masoe">Maselino Masoe</a>, who represented American Samoa in three consecutive <a href="/wiki/Olympic_Games" title="Olympic Games">Olympics</a> from 1988 to 1996, was <a href="/wiki/World_Boxing_Association" title="World Boxing Association">WBA</a> <a href="/wiki/List_of_WBA_world_champions" title="List of WBA world champions">middleweight champion</a> from 2004 to 2006.</li>
<li><b>Professional wrestling</b>: Several American Samoan athletes have been very visible in <a href="/wiki/Professional_wrestling" title="Professional wrestling">professional wrestling</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Anoa%27i_family" title="Anoa&#39;i family">Anoa'i family</a> in particular has had many of its members employed by <a href="/wiki/WWE" title="WWE">WWE</a>.</li>
<li><b>Sumo wrestling</b>: Some Samoan <a href="/wiki/Sumo" title="Sumo">Sumo</a> wrestlers, most famously <a href="/wiki/Musashimaru" class="mw-redirect" title="Musashimaru">Musashimaru</a> and <a href="/wiki/Konishiki" class="mw-redirect" title="Konishiki">Konishiki</a>, have reached the highest ranks of <i><a href="/wiki/Makuuchi#Ōzeki" title="Makuuchi">ōzeki</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Yokozuna_(sumo)" class="mw-redirect" title="Yokozuna (sumo)">yokozuna</a></i>.</li>
<li><b>Track and field</b>: <a href="/wiki/Hammer_throw" title="Hammer throw">Hammer thrower</a> <a href="/wiki/Lisa_Misipeka" title="Lisa Misipeka">Lisa Misipeka</a> attracted international attention by winning a bronze medal in the <a href="/wiki/1999_World_Championships_in_Athletics" title="1999 World Championships in Athletics">1999 World Championships in Athletics</a>.</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Recreation">Recreation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: Recreation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pola_Islands_Tutuila_NPS.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Pola_Islands_Tutuila_NPS.jpg/220px-Pola_Islands_Tutuila_NPS.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Pola_Islands_Tutuila_NPS.jpg/330px-Pola_Islands_Tutuila_NPS.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3c/Pola_Islands_Tutuila_NPS.jpg/440px-Pola_Islands_Tutuila_NPS.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2576" data-file-height="1932" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pola_Islands_Tutuila_NPS.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Pola_Island" title="Pola Island">Pola Island</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Aunu%27u_Island_National_National_Landmark.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Aunu%27u_Island_National_National_Landmark.jpg/220px-Aunu%27u_Island_National_National_Landmark.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/c4/Aunu%27u_Island_National_National_Landmark.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="225" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Aunu%27u_Island_National_National_Landmark.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Aunuʻu Island</div></div></div>
<p>A team from the <a href="/wiki/Bureau_of_Outdoor_Recreation" title="Bureau of Outdoor Recreation">Bureau of Outdoor Recreation</a> conducted a parks survey on American Samoa in the 1960s. Their team recommended sites at <a href="/wiki/Cape_Taputapu" title="Cape Taputapu">Cape Taputapu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Le%27ala_Shoreline" title="Le&#39;ala Shoreline">Leʻala</a> at <a href="/wiki/Vailoatai,_American_Samoa" title="Vailoatai, American Samoa">Vailoatai</a>, <a href="/wiki/A%27oloau" title="A&#39;oloau">Aʻoloau Fou</a> (the plateau), Matautuloa Point, <a href="/wiki/Nu%CA%BBuuli" class="mw-redirect" title="Nuʻuuli">Nuʻuuli</a>, <a href="/wiki/Matafao_Peak" title="Matafao Peak">Matafao Peak</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago" title="Pago Pago">Pago Pago</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vai%CA%BBava_Strait" title="Vaiʻava Strait">Vaiʻava Strait</a>, Anasosopo, <a href="/wiki/%27Aoa" title="&#39;Aoa">ʻAoa</a>, Cape Matautuloa, and <a href="/wiki/Aunu%CA%BBu" title="Aunuʻu">Aunuʻu Island</a>. After an initial objection, Secretary Leʻiato gave his support and was appointed Chairman of the Territorial Parks and Recreation Committee. The first field meeting for a parkland acquisition was held between Judge Morrow on behalf of the government and the village council of <a href="/wiki/Vatia,_American_Samoa" title="Vatia, American Samoa">Vatia</a> to make the <a href="/wiki/Pola_Island" title="Pola Island">Pola Island</a> area a public park. The dredge <i>Palolo</i> was hired from <a href="/wiki/Upolu" title="Upolu">Upolu</a> in January 1966 in order to dredge sand for <a href="/wiki/Utulei" class="mw-redirect" title="Utulei">Utulei Beach</a>. A specialist in beach developments, Ala Varone of the Army, directed the project. The centerpiece of the park was to be at the head of <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago_Harbor" title="Pago Pago Harbor">Pago Pago Harbor</a>, where it proposed a 13-acre site created by the dredge. The park would have facilities for sports and recreation as well as facilities for boats and the growing number of Asian immigrants arriving from Korea, Japan, and China.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 285">&#58;&#8202;285&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>The Department of Parks and Recreation was created by law in 1980 and the Parks Commission was also established.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 315">&#58;&#8202;315&#8202;</span></sup> In 1981, Governor <a href="/wiki/Peter_Tali_Coleman" title="Peter Tali Coleman">Peter Tali Coleman</a> appointed Fuga Tolani Teleso as Director of Parks and Recreation. On May 25, 1984, a groundbreaking ceremony was held at the Onesosopo reclamation to initiate work on the first park in the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_District,_American_Samoa" title="Eastern District, American Samoa">Eastern District</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 332">&#58;&#8202;332&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>At the urging of Dr. Paul Cox, High Chief Nafanua of <a href="/wiki/Falealupo" title="Falealupo">Falealupo</a>, and the Bat Preservers Association, Congressman <a href="/wiki/Fof%C5%8D_Iosefa_Fiti_Sunia" title="Fofō Iosefa Fiti Sunia">Fofō Iosefa Fiti Sunia</a> introduced a bill in 1984 which would enter American Samoa into the Federal Fish and Wildlife Restoration Act. The purpose of the bill was to protect the ancient <a href="/wiki/Paleotropical" class="mw-redirect" title="Paleotropical">paleotropical</a> rainforests and the <a href="/wiki/Flying_fox" class="mw-redirect" title="Flying fox">Flying fox</a> <a href="/wiki/Megabat" title="Megabat">megabat</a>. The signing marked the beginning of American Samoa's entry into the U.S. <a href="/wiki/National_Park_System" class="mw-redirect" title="National Park System">National Park System</a>. In July 1987, the National Park Service began establishing a federal park, the <a href="/wiki/National_Park_of_American_Samoa" title="National Park of American Samoa">National Park of American Samoa</a>. An initial appropriation of $400,000 was made in 1989. It contains one of the world's most remarkable rainforest and coastal reef ecologies and spreads across three islands. One of the most popular sites on <a href="/wiki/Tutuila" title="Tutuila">Tutuila Island</a> include Pola Rock, a rise of sheer rock formations that protrudes over 400<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>feet (120<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>m) above the ocean's surface. It is located off the shores of <a href="/wiki/Vatia,_American_Samoa" title="Vatia, American Samoa">Vatia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 332">&#58;&#8202;332&#8202;</span></sup> On September 19, 1991, Governor <a href="/wiki/Peter_Tali_Coleman" title="Peter Tali Coleman">Peter Tali Coleman</a> and <a href="/wiki/Department_of_the_Interior" class="mw-redirect" title="Department of the Interior">Department of the Interior</a> secretary <a href="/wiki/Manuel_Lujan" class="mw-redirect" title="Manuel Lujan">Manuel Lujan</a> signed leases formalizing the establishment of the fiftieth U.S. National Park.<sup id="cite_ref-Sunia-2009_22-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sunia-2009-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 335">&#58;&#8202;335&#8202;</span></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:American_Samoa_National_Park_sign_for_natural_landmarks.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/American_Samoa_National_Park_sign_for_natural_landmarks.jpg/220px-American_Samoa_National_Park_sign_for_natural_landmarks.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/American_Samoa_National_Park_sign_for_natural_landmarks.jpg/330px-American_Samoa_National_Park_sign_for_natural_landmarks.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a4/American_Samoa_National_Park_sign_for_natural_landmarks.jpg/440px-American_Samoa_National_Park_sign_for_natural_landmarks.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5312" data-file-height="2988" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:American_Samoa_National_Park_sign_for_natural_landmarks.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>National Natural Landmarks</div></div></div>
<p>The ASG Parks and Recreation oversees the maintenance of all public parks, including the <a href="/wiki/Amanave" class="mw-redirect" title="Amanave">Amanave</a> Mini Park, Lions Park in <a href="/wiki/Tafuna,_American_Samoa" title="Tafuna, American Samoa">Tafuna</a>, Onesosopo Park in <a href="/wiki/Aua,_American_Samoa" title="Aua, American Samoa">Aua</a>, Malaloa Mini Park, <a href="/wiki/Faga%27alu" class="mw-redirect" title="Faga&#39;alu">Fagaʻalu</a> Park, <a href="/wiki/Tia_Seu_Lupe" title="Tia Seu Lupe">Tia Seu Lupe</a> historical site at <a href="/wiki/Tafuna,_American_Samoa" title="Tafuna, American Samoa">Fatuoaiga</a>, Pago Pago Park, Pago Pago Tennis Courts, the Little League Softball Field, Tony Solaʻita Baseball Field, Solo Ridge at the Utulei Tramway, Utulei Beach Park and Suʻigaulaoleatuvasa in <a href="/wiki/Utulei,_American_Samoa" title="Utulei, American Samoa">Utulei</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Matafao_Peak_National_Natural_Landmark.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9c/Matafao_Peak_National_Natural_Landmark.jpg/220px-Matafao_Peak_National_Natural_Landmark.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/9c/Matafao_Peak_National_Natural_Landmark.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="300" data-file-height="225" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Matafao_Peak_National_Natural_Landmark.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Matafao_Peak" title="Matafao Peak">Matafao Peak</a> National Natural Landmark</div></div></div>
<p>American Samoa has seven areas designated as National Natural Landmarks on Tutuila Island. This program is administrated by the U.S. National Park Service and the areas contain unique ecological or geological features. Except <a href="/wiki/Vai%CA%BBava_Strait" title="Vaiʻava Strait">Vaiʻava Strait</a>, none of the areas are within the National Park of American Samoa.<sup id="cite_ref-Goldin-2002_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goldin-2002-188">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 281">&#58;&#8202;281&#8202;</span></sup> American Samoa's seven <a href="/wiki/National_Natural_Landmark" title="National Natural Landmark">National Natural Landmarks</a> (NNL) were designated in 1972:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Cape_Taputapu" title="Cape Taputapu">Cape Taputapu</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Fogama%27a_Crater" title="Fogama&#39;a Crater">Fogāmaʻa Crater</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Matafao_Peak" title="Matafao Peak">Matafao Peak</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Le%27ala_Shoreline" title="Le&#39;ala Shoreline">Leʻala Shoreline</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Rainmaker_Mountain" title="Rainmaker Mountain">Rainmaker Mountain</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Vai%CA%BBava_Strait" title="Vaiʻava Strait">Vaiʻava Strait</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Aunu%CA%BBu" title="Aunuʻu">Aunuʻu Island</a></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Wildlife">Wildlife</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=48" title="Edit section: Wildlife">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Notable terrestrial species include the <a href="/wiki/Candoia_bibroni" title="Candoia bibroni">Pacific tree boa</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Samoa_flying_fox" title="Samoa flying fox">Samoa flying fox</a>, which has a three-foot wingspread.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup> Two snake species can be found in American Samoa: The <a href="/wiki/Indotyphlops_braminus" title="Indotyphlops braminus">Brahminy blind snake</a> is found on Tutuila, while the Pacific tree boa occurs on Taʻū. The islands are home to five species of geckos: <a href="/wiki/Pacific_slender-toed_gecko" class="mw-redirect" title="Pacific slender-toed gecko">Pacific slender-toed gecko</a>, <a href="/wiki/Oceanic_gecko" class="mw-redirect" title="Oceanic gecko">Oceanic gecko</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mourning_gecko" class="mw-redirect" title="Mourning gecko">Mourning gecko</a>, <a href="/wiki/Stump-toed_gecko" class="mw-redirect" title="Stump-toed gecko">Stump-toed gecko</a>, and <a href="/wiki/House_gecko" class="mw-redirect" title="House gecko">House gecko</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-nathist_190-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nathist-190">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Goldin-2002_188-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goldin-2002-188">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 253">&#58;&#8202;253&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>Turtles include the threatened <a href="/wiki/Green_sea_turtle" title="Green sea turtle">Green sea turtle</a> and the endangered <a href="/wiki/Hawksbill_sea_turtle" title="Hawksbill sea turtle">Hawksbill sea turtle</a>. Hawksbill sea turtles tend to nest on Tutuila beaches, while the Green sea turtle is most common on <a href="/wiki/Rose_Atoll" title="Rose Atoll">Rose Atoll</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> Tutuila has the highest number of nesting turtles, consisting of around fifty nesting females per year.<sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>American Samoa is home to one species of amphibian: the <a href="/wiki/Cane_toad" title="Cane toad">Cane toad</a>. Biologists estimate that there are over two million toads on Tutuila.<sup id="cite_ref-Goldin-2002_188-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goldin-2002-188">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 252">&#58;&#8202;252&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>915 nearshore fish species have been recorded in American Samoa, compared to only 460 nearshore fish species in <a href="/wiki/Hawaii" title="Hawaii">Hawaii</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Goldin-2002_188-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goldin-2002-188">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 20">&#58;&#8202;20&#8202;</span></sup> With over 950 species of native fish and 250 coral species, American Samoa has the greatest <a href="/wiki/Marine_biodiversity" class="mw-redirect" title="Marine biodiversity">marine biodiversity</a> in the United States.<sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Fruit_bats">Fruit bats</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=49" title="Edit section: Fruit bats">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Pteropus_samoensis2-2-2.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Pteropus_samoensis2-2-2.jpg/220px-Pteropus_samoensis2-2-2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="330" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Pteropus_samoensis2-2-2.jpg/330px-Pteropus_samoensis2-2-2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Pteropus_samoensis2-2-2.jpg/440px-Pteropus_samoensis2-2-2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="952" data-file-height="1428" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Pteropus_samoensis2-2-2.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Samoa_flying_fox" title="Samoa flying fox">Samoa flying fox</a> is only found in Fiji and the Samoan Islands.</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Megabat" title="Megabat">Megabats</a> are the only native mammal in American Samoa. The islands are home to two species of fruit bats: <a href="/wiki/Insular_flying_fox" title="Insular flying fox">Pacific flying fox</a> and <a href="/wiki/Samoa_flying_fox" title="Samoa flying fox">Samoa flying fox</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Sheath-tailed_bat" class="mw-redirect" title="Sheath-tailed bat">Sheath-tailed bat</a> is another species found here, which is a smaller insect-eating bat. In 1992, the American Samoa Government banned the hunting of fruit bats to help their populations recover.<sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup> The Samoa flying fox is only found in <a href="/wiki/Fiji" title="Fiji">Fiji</a> and the Samoan Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-nathist_190-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nathist-190">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Goldin-2002_188-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goldin-2002-188">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 200">&#58;&#8202;200&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>From 1995 to 2000, the population of Samoa flying fox remained stable at about 900 animals on Tutuila, and 100 in the <a href="/wiki/Manu%CA%BBa_Islands" title="Manuʻa Islands">Manuʻa Islands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup> As of the year 2000, scientists from the American Samoa Department of Marine and Wildlife Resource estimated that there are fewer than 5,500 Pacific flying foxes in American Samoa, and an estimated 900 or fewer Samoa flying foxes.<sup id="cite_ref-Goldin-2002_188-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goldin-2002-188">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 199">&#58;&#8202;199&#8202;</span></sup> The best and biggest known <a href="/wiki/Roosting" class="mw-redirect" title="Roosting">roost</a> on Tutuila Island for the Sheath-tailed bat is in the Anapeʻape Cove near <a href="/wiki/Afono,_American_Samoa" class="mw-redirect" title="Afono, American Samoa">Āfono</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Amalau_Valley" title="Amalau Valley">Amalau Valley</a> on Tutuila's north coast offers great roadside views of many bird species and both species of fruit bat.<sup id="cite_ref-Goldin-2002_188-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Goldin-2002-188">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: 274">&#58;&#8202;274&#8202;</span></sup> The valley has been called a prime bird- and bat-watching area.<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Avifauna">Avifauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=50" title="Edit section: Avifauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Vini_australis_-London_Zoo,_England-8a.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Vini_australis_-London_Zoo%2C_England-8a.jpg/220px-Vini_australis_-London_Zoo%2C_England-8a.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Vini_australis_-London_Zoo%2C_England-8a.jpg/330px-Vini_australis_-London_Zoo%2C_England-8a.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Vini_australis_-London_Zoo%2C_England-8a.jpg/440px-Vini_australis_-London_Zoo%2C_England-8a.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3648" data-file-height="2736" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Vini_australis_-London_Zoo,_England-8a.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Blue-crowned_lorikeet" title="Blue-crowned lorikeet">Blue-crowned lorikeet</a> is the only parrot found in American Samoa.</div></div></div>
<p>Sixteen of the Samoan Islands' 34 bird species are found nowhere else on Earth. This includes the critically endangered <a href="/wiki/Tooth-billed_pigeon" title="Tooth-billed pigeon">tooth-billed pigeon</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup> Four species of birds are only found in the Manuʻa Islands and not on Tutuila. These include American Samoa's only parrot, the <a href="/wiki/Blue-crowned_Lory" class="mw-redirect" title="Blue-crowned Lory">blue-crowned Lory</a>. Other special birds to Manuʻa are the <a href="/wiki/Lesser_shrikebill" class="mw-redirect" title="Lesser shrikebill">lesser shrikebill</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Friendly_ground-dove" class="mw-redirect" title="Friendly ground-dove">friendly ground-dove</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Spotless_crake" title="Spotless crake">spotless crake</a> has only been observed on Taʻū Island.<sup id="cite_ref-nathist_190-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nathist-190">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are more species of birds than all species of reptiles, mammals and amphibians combined. Native land birds include two honeyeaters: <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_honeyeater" class="mw-redirect" title="Cardinal honeyeater">cardinal honeyeater</a> and <a href="/wiki/Wattled_honeyeater" title="Wattled honeyeater">wattled honeyeater</a>. Cardinal honeyeaters only occur on Tutuila Island. The only endemic land bird to American Samoa is the <a href="/wiki/Samoan_starling" title="Samoan starling">Samoan starling</a>. Four pigeons are native to American Samoa: <a href="/wiki/Pacific_imperial_pigeon" title="Pacific imperial pigeon">Pacific imperial pigeon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Many-colored_fruit_dove" title="Many-colored fruit dove">many-colored fruit dove</a>, <a href="/wiki/White-capped_fruit_dove" title="White-capped fruit dove">white-capped fruit dove</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Shy_ground_dove" class="mw-redirect" title="Shy ground dove">shy ground dove</a>. The local government banned all pigeon hunting in 1992.<sup id="cite_ref-201" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-201">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The many-colored fruit dove is one of the rarest birds that nest on Tutuila. Studies in the 1980s estimated their population size at Tutuila to be only around 80 birds.<sup id="cite_ref-nathist_190-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nathist-190">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Amalau_Valley" title="Amalau Valley">Amalau Valley</a> has been described as the best place in American Samoa to observe the many-colored fruit dove.<sup id="cite_ref-Watling-Rinke-2001_202-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Watling-Rinke-2001-202">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The offshore islet of <a href="/wiki/Pola_Island" title="Pola Island">Pola Island</a> near <a href="/wiki/Vatia,_American_Samoa" title="Vatia, American Samoa">Vatia</a> is a nesting site for many seabird species and an excellent area to observe seabirds.<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Watling-Rinke-2001_202-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Watling-Rinke-2001-202">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> The Pola region of Vatia and <a href="/wiki/Rose_Atoll" title="Rose Atoll">Rose Atoll</a> are the only places in American Samoa where there are breeding colonies of <a href="/wiki/Red-footed_boobies" class="mw-redirect" title="Red-footed boobies">red-footed boobies</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Birds that depend on freshwater habitat include the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_reef_heron" title="Pacific reef heron">Pacific reef heron</a> and <a href="/wiki/Pacific_black_duck" title="Pacific black duck">Pacific black duck</a>, the Samoan Islands' only species of duck. The largest wetland areas are the pala lagoons in <a href="/wiki/Nu%27uuli,_American_Samoa" title="Nu&#39;uuli, American Samoa">Nuʻuuli</a> and <a href="/wiki/Leone,_American_Samoa" title="Leone, American Samoa">Leone</a> as well as Pala Lake on <a href="/wiki/Aunu%CA%BBu" title="Aunuʻu">Aunuʻu</a> Island.<sup id="cite_ref-nathist_190-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nathist-190">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=51" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<li class="portalbox-entry"><span class="portalbox-image"><a href="/wiki/File:%E5%A4%A7%E6%B4%8B.png" class="image"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/%E5%A4%A7%E6%B4%8B.png/32px-%E5%A4%A7%E6%B4%8B.png" decoding="async" width="32" height="26" class="noviewer" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/%E5%A4%A7%E6%B4%8B.png/48px-%E5%A4%A7%E6%B4%8B.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/%E5%A4%A7%E6%B4%8B.png/64px-%E5%A4%A7%E6%B4%8B.png 2x" data-file-width="756" data-file-height="617" /></a></span><span class="portalbox-link"><a href="/wiki/Portal:Oceania" title="Portal:Oceania">Oceania portal</a></span></li><li class="portalbox-entry"><span class="portalbox-image"><img alt="flag" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/a4/Flag_of_the_United_States.svg/32px-Flag_of_the_United_States.svg.png" decoding="async" width="32" height="17" class="noviewer thumbborder" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/a4/Flag_of_the_United_States.svg/48px-Flag_of_the_United_States.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/a4/Flag_of_the_United_States.svg/64px-Flag_of_the_United_States.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1235" data-file-height="650" /></span><span class="portalbox-link"><a href="/wiki/Portal:United_States" title="Portal:United States">United States portal</a></span></li></ul>

<li><a href="/wiki/Polynesia" title="Polynesia">Polynesia</a></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=American_Samoa&amp;action=edit&amp;section=52" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-1"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-1">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">American Samoa belongs to, but is not a part of, the United States. See the page for the <a href="/wiki/Insular_Cases#Background" title="Insular Cases">Insular Cases</a> for more information.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-capital-2"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-capital_2-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-capital_2-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-capital_2-2"><sup><i><b>c</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text">The constitution specifies the seat of government at <a href="/wiki/Fagatogo" title="Fagatogo">Fagatogo</a>, where the <a href="/wiki/American_Samoa_Fono" title="American Samoa Fono">legislature</a>, <a href="/wiki/High_Court_of_American_Samoa" title="High Court of American Samoa">High Court</a> and District Court are located.<sup id="cite_ref-constitution_57-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitution-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-highcourt_63-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-highcourt-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-districtcourt_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-districtcourt-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-noaa_61-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-noaa-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> The executive office building is located in neighboring <a href="/wiki/Utulei,_American_Samoa" title="Utulei, American Samoa">Utulei</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-executive_59-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-executive-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-npsmap_60-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-npsmap-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> These two villages are located along <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago_Harbor" title="Pago Pago Harbor">Pago Pago Harbor</a>, whose largest village is <a href="/wiki/Pago_Pago" title="Pago Pago">Pago Pago</a>. Many sources list Pago Pago as the capital, referring to the whole agglomeration around the harbor.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-CIAfactbook_3-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAfactbook-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-87"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-87">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">To travel to American Samoa, U.S. nationals need to show proof of existing residence or future employment in American Samoa, or a ticket for future departure from the territory.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> However, once there, U.S. nationals may reside indefinitely and cannot be deported.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-107"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-107">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">An American Samoan law of 1962 defined 14 counties.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> The constitution of 1967, signed by delegates from these 14 counties, established 15 counties from then on, separating Fofo from Lealataua.<sup id="cite_ref-constitution_57-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitution-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> The election law was later revised accordingly.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> However, the U.S. Census Bureau continues to list 14 counties, treating Fofo as part of Lealataua.<sup id="cite_ref-census-counties_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census-counties-4">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<li id="cite_note-13"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-13">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFStanton1975" class="citation book cs1">Stanton, William (1975). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.org/details/greatunitedstate00will/page/132"><i>The Great United States Exploring Expedition</i></a>. <li id="cite_note-14"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-14">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFStevenson,_Robert_Louis1892" class="citation book cs1">Stevenson, Robert Louis (1892). <a href="/wiki/A_Footnote_to_History:_Eight_Years_of_Trouble_in_Samoa" title="A Footnote to History: Eight Years of Trouble in Samoa"><i>A Footnote to History: Eight Years of Trouble in Samoa</i></a>. <li id="cite_note-75"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-75">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.gao.gov/new.items/d10638.pdf">"America Samoa: Performing a Risk Assessment Would Better Inform U.S. Agencies of the Risks Related to Acceptance of Certificates of Identity"</a> <span class="cs1-format">(PDF)</span>. <a href="/wiki/Government_Accountability_Office" title="Government Accountability Office">U.S. Government Accountability Office</a>. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">September 21,</span> 2014</span>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=America+Samoa%3A+Performing+a+Risk+Assessment+Would+Better+Inform+U.S.+Agencies+of+the+Risks+Related+to+Acceptance+of+Certificates+of+Identity&amp;rft.pages=11+%28p.+15+of+the+pdf%29&amp;rft.pub=U.S.+Government+Accountability+Office&amp;rft.date=2010-06&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.gao.gov%2Fnew.items%2Fd10638.pdf&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AAmerican+Samoa" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<li id="cite_note-76"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-76">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation cs2"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20150923174350/http://www.asbar.org/images/legal_issues/citizenship/tuaua%20v%20%20us%20faleomavaega%20amicus%20brief.pdf"><i>Amicus Curiae Brief of Eni F. H. Faleomavaega</i></a> <span class="cs1-format">(PDF)</span>, November 7, 2012, archived from <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.asbar.org/images/legal_issues/citizenship/tuaua%20v%20%20us%20faleomavaega%20amicus%20brief.pdf">the original</a> <span class="cs1-format">(PDF)</span> on September 23, 2015<span class="reference-accessdate">, retrieved <span class="nowrap">April 26,</span> 2014</span>, <q>More than a century ago, the Supreme Court held that the Citizenship Clause of the Fourteenth Amendment does not extend birthright citizenship to United States nationals who are born in unincorporated territories. See Downes v. Bidwell, 182 US 244, 251 (1901). The Court has reaffirmed this principle through the years, noting that individuals who are born in an unincorporated territory, though "subject to the jurisdiction of the United States," are "American nationals" who are not birthright citizens of the United States. Barber v. Gonzales, 347 U.S. 637, 639 n.1 (1954).</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Amicus+Curiae+Brief+of+Eni+F.+H.+Faleomavaega&amp;rft.date=2012-11-07&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.asbar.org%2Fimages%2Flegal_issues%2Fcitizenship%2Ftuaua%2520v%2520%2520us%2520faleomavaega%2520amicus%2520brief.pdf&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AAmerican+Samoa" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-77"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-77">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://harvardlawreview.org/2017/04/american-samoa-and-the-citizenship-clause/">American Samoa and the Citizenship Clause: A Study in Insular Cases Revisionism</a>. Chapter 3. Harvard Law Review. Retrieved 7 January 2018.</span>
